**Codified fix:** A count change in a gate document requires a corpus-wide grep for the old count literal (in context) before declaring the fix complete. The Defensive Sweep Discipline (S-7.02) applies to gate-internal prose as much as to cross-document count fields.
**Applicable to:** All gate-authoring bursts that modify a count or enumeration.

### L-048 [codified]: AN EXEMPTION LIST MUST BE VALIDATED AGAINST ARCHITECTURE DOCS FOR EVERY NAMED MEMBER

**Discovered:** P1D-172a / F-P172a-04 (`memory::skills` definitions-only exemption, 2026-07-25)
**Symptom:** Burst-273 granted `memory::skills` a definitions-only carve-out from criticality-registry requirements, stating the criterion "hosts ONLY type/trait definitions, no I/O." `purity-boundary-map.md` §Effectful Shell places `memory::skills` with the description "async `SkillStore` I/O: reads skill KV entries via `MemoryStore` backend." The exemption's own prerequisite is refuted by the authoritative purity map. The defect was introduced in fix-burst 273 under orchestrator routing.
**Root cause:** The specialist authoring the exemption list did not cross-check each named member against `purity-boundary-map.md`. Exemption eligibility requires verifying all three sources: `module-decomposition.md` (definitions note), `purity-boundary-map.md` (tier row), and the module's BCs (I/O obligations).
**Codified fix:** Any exemption list granting a module a "definitions-only" or "no-row" status MUST include a cross-check of that module's `purity-boundary-map.md` tier row and its BCs for I/O obligations, in the same authoring burst.
**Applicable to:** All gate #32 exemption authoring. Extend to any future "carve-out" or "non-violation" class creation.

### L-049 [codified]: A CENSUS WITH A PERMANENT FALSE FAILURE IS WORSE THAN NO CENSUS

**Discovered:** P1D-172a / F-P172a-13 (gate #36 VP-INDEX false failure, 2026-07-25)
**Symptom:** Gate #36's census `grep -rL "^red_gate:" specs/verification-properties/VP-*.md` matches `VP-INDEX.md` (an index file with no `red_gate:` field), so the command can NEVER return the expected "empty" output. The gate perpetually reports an apparent violation even when the corpus is clean. Operators who see this persistent apparent failure will discard the gate as broken — which is worse than not having the gate at all.
**Root cause:** Gate authoring did not verify that the glob pattern excludes co-located index and template files.
**Codified fix:** When a gate uses a glob (`VP-*.md`, `BC-*.md`, etc.), verify that the glob excludes co-located index files. Use `VP-[0-9][0-9][0-9].md` patterns or add `| grep -v INDEX` filters. Any "expected: empty" assertion must be verifiable in a clean corpus BEFORE the gate is committed.
**Applicable to:** All gate census commands with "expected: empty" assertions and glob file selectors.

### L-050 [codified]: REGISTRY/ENUMERATION LISTS INSIDE GATES DECAY SILENTLY

**Discovered:** P1D-172a / F-P172a-07/08 (stale supplement enumeration + stale "95 BCs" at six sites, 2026-07-25)
**Symptom:** Gate #28 Rule 5's supplement enumeration hardcodes five files while seven live supplements exist — `observability.md` was added in burst-258 without updating gate #28. Six authoring-guideline sites still read "95 BCs" against a corpus of 129; the same three sites swept 86→95 by v2.13 were not re-swept 95→129 by v2.42.
**Root cause:** Hardcoded enumerations inside gates are not protected by any invariant. When the corpus changes, the gate does not fail — it silently becomes inaccurate.
**Codified fix:** Prefer corpus-derived census commands over hardcoded file lists. When a new document is added to any enumerated category, a MANDATORY sibling-sweep step must check all gates that enumerate that category.
**Applicable to:** All gate enumerations. Treat hardcoded file lists in gate text as technical debt that will become a false-negative.

### L-051 [codified]: A BROKEN GATE HIDES EVERYTHING IT WAS BUILT TO FIND

**Discovered:** burst-274 (P1D-172a census repairs → 18-module criticality gap discovered, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** Repairing gate #33's column-map (field-index `$4`→`$5`) and gate #25 Part C's `awk` pipeline immediately made 18 missing criticality rows visible for the first time. No adversarial pass in 172 passes had reported the gap — because the census commands that would have detected it could not execute. A broken gate does not produce false negatives; it produces no signal at all, which is indistinguishable from "all clear."
**Root cause:** Enforcement mechanisms were trusted as silent validators. Gate health was never independently audited. When a gate's census command is structurally broken, its output is meaningless regardless of how many adversarial passes invoke it.
**Codified fix:** Before trusting any gate's "no violations" output, verify the gate's census commands execute correctly by dry-running them against the current corpus. A gate that cannot enumerate its target population cannot certify it. Gate health audits belong on the adversary's standing probe list.
**Applicable to:** All factory gates with census commands. Priority on gates that declare "expected: empty" or "Zero X permitted" — these are the easiest to produce a false pass from.

### L-052 [codified]: BACKFILLS SCOPED BY ONE ATTRIBUTE SILENTLY OMIT EVERYTHING LACKING IT

**Discovered:** burst-274 (root cause of 18-module criticality gap, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** The D21+burst-224 criticality backfill was scoped to modules that had a VP. Every execution-logic module without a VP was systematically skipped — not just D21, but in every backfill generation that used the same scoping criterion. The scoping error propagated silently across multiple bursts because no adversary could check for missing rows in a registry it did not know was incomplete.
**Root cause:** "Scope the backfill to entities that have property X" is a heuristic, not a correctness criterion. Modules without a VP are not exempt from criticality classification; they were simply not enumerated by the scoping criterion. Completeness of the registry was never verified independently of the backfill's internal logic.
**Codified fix:** After any bulk backfill, the completeness criterion must be derived from the AUTHORITATIVE ROSTER (module-decomposition.md), not from a property of the entities being backfilled. Verify: every module in the roster either has a criticality row OR has an explicit definitions-only annotation. Missing entries are defects, not acceptable gaps.
**Applicable to:** All bulk backfill operations for any registry. The completeness check must enumerate from the source roster, not from the populated registry.

### L-053 [codified]: INTENTIONAL ABSENCE MUST BE ANNOTATED

**Discovered:** burst-274 (exempt modules indistinguishable from forgotten modules, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** `core::documents` and `memory::skills` were legitimately exempt from the criticality registry (one is a pure data carrier, one is a routing-overlay). But they were listed without any annotation in `module-decomposition.md`, making them visually indistinguishable from the 18 modules that were genuinely missing. An adversary reviewing `module-criticality.md` could not distinguish "intentionally omitted per ADR decision" from "accidentally omitted."
**Root cause:** Exemptions were applied silently. The absence of a row was the only signal, and absence is ambiguous. Without an annotation, every future reviewer must re-adjudicate the question "is this missing by design or by oversight?"
**Codified fix:** Every module in `module-decomposition.md` must have either: (a) a criticality row in `module-criticality.md`, or (b) an explicit exemption annotation in `module-decomposition.md`'s Criticality column (e.g., `—` with a parenthetical reason citing the governing ADR decision). Silent absence is a defect. Verify at every module-addition burst.
**Applicable to:** All modules in the registry. The annotation discipline extends to any future registry where intentional absence needs to be distinguished from error.

### L-054 [codified]: A MECHANICAL GATE'S SHORT-CIRCUIT IS A SILENT COVERAGE HOLE

**Discovered:** burst-274 (F-P172a-14, both-forms validator gap, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** Both changelog validators (`verify-changelog-date-monotonicity.sh` and `verify-form-a-changelog-direction.sh`) checked for Form A (frontmatter changelog list) first and returned early if it was found. The per-file loop never reached the Form-B body-table branch. Four both-forms files (`BC-INDEX.md`, `ubiquitous-language-server.md`, `bc-authoring-plan.md`, `test-vectors.md`) had Form-B content that was never validated — and Form-B version rows in those files went unmonitored across all prior bursts.
**Root cause:** Early-return logic in validators is a coverage hazard. When a file can have multiple valid forms of the same content (frontmatter AND body table), checking one and returning prevents the other from being checked. The validator appeared to PASS for these files while silently skipping half the validation.
**Codified fix:** Validators that check multiple forms of the same artifact must evaluate ALL applicable forms independently before moving to the next file. Early-return on "found Form A" is a coverage gap, not a success signal. When minting or extending a validator, explicitly test it against files that have BOTH forms to verify both branches run.
**Applicable to:** All multi-form validators. Test harness entries for both-forms files are now required at minting time.

### L-055 [codified]: "OUTSIDE THIS BURST'S SCOPE" IS NOT A VALID DEFERRAL WHEN THE DEFECT IS IN THE SAME FILES BEING EDITED

**Discovered:** burst-274 (18-module criticality sweep initially deferred, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** During burst-274's Wave C, the corpus-wide criticality sweep was initially proposed as a separate future burst ("out of scope for this burst"). The gap was identified while the architect was already editing `module-criticality.md` and `module-decomposition.md`. The production-grade default was applied: if you find a defect while editing a file, fix it now; "future burst" is a defer-pattern when the work is already in scope.
**Root cause:** Scope boundaries feel like a safety mechanism but become an evasion mechanism when the gap is in the same files being edited. "Out of scope" requires that the fix genuinely requires new specs or new architecture decisions — not merely more rows in a file already open.
**Codified fix:** When a specialist is already editing a file and finds additional defects of the same class in that file, the default is to fix them in the current burst. A deferral requires: (a) the fix genuinely requires work in a different domain (new BC, new ADR), AND (b) explicit human direction or orchestrator approval. "It would take more time" is not a valid deferral reason.
**Applicable to:** All fix-bursts. The production-grade default §Rule 4 applies: AI-found defects in AI-authored content default to in-scope fix.

### L-056 [codified]: NEVER DIFF AGAINST A DERIVED FIGURE YOU HAVE NOT RECOUNTED

**Discovered:** P1D-172b / F-P172b-02 (phantom "56-module universe" baseline, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** Burst-274's entire criticality sweep was gated on a "56-module universe" that had never equaled the artifact it named. An independent recount yields 70 rows (68 tiered + 2 exempt). The figure had been mirroring the criticality registry total since v1.2, not the decomposition row count — at v1.11 it recorded 35 while the actual table already held 48 tiered rows. The arithmetic was impossible on its face: a 48-row registry cannot yield 18 gaps against a 56-row universe.
**Root cause:** The baseline figure "56" was accepted at face value from an earlier prose claim without being derived from the artifact it names. No step in the sweep procedure required verifying that the baseline was actually computed from `module-decomposition.md`'s row count.
**Proposed fix:** Any sweep that computes a delta (registry_count vs universe_count) must recount the universe from the authoritative source in the same burst, not from a cached prose claim. The coverage triple (decomposition_tiered_rows, registry_rows, matched_rows) must be recorded in the burst changelog and the arithmetic must check out before the sweep is declared complete.
**Applicable to:** All census operations with a "universe" or "baseline" figure.

### L-057 [codified]: AN EXEMPTION CLAUSE CAN INVERT THE CHECK IT GUARDS

**Discovered:** P1D-172b / F-P172b-05 (gate #25 Part B exemption clause inversion, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** The gate #25 Part B exemption added in fix-burst 274 ended "Only check modules that are present as rows in the arch-registry (module-criticality.md) table." This inverted the check direction: a module with a tier in `module-decomposition.md` but no registry row was silently skipped without being flagged, exempt-listed, or noted. This is exactly the class the same burst was closing. The clause was introduced by orchestrator routing within the burst.
**Root cause:** The exemption clause was phrased as a constraint on the iteration domain ("only check modules that ARE in the registry"), when it should have been phrased as a permission to skip specific named members. The two framings look similar but have opposite effects on gap detection.
**Proposed fix:** Exemptions must always name WHAT is exempt (specific named members), never narrow the iteration universe. The correct form is: "Iterate all modules in `module-decomposition.md` with a non-`—` Criticality value. For each: a registry row MUST exist, UNLESS the module is in this explicit exempt list." The iteration domain is always the authoritative roster; exemptions are exceptions to the rule, not filters on the population.
**Applicable to:** All gate exemption clauses that might affect iteration domain. Review at minting and after every exemption-list edit.

### L-058 [codified]: A CENSUS THAT COMPARES STRINGS REQUIRES ONE NAMING CONVENTION

**Discovered:** P1D-172b / F-P172b-06 (mixed naming conventions, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** Burst-274 introduced `crate::module` naming into a registry that was ~45% prose-named, leaving ~30 of 66 rows unmatchable by gate #25 Part C's exact-string census. The table now holds `sqlite backend` alongside `checkpoint::memory`, `checkpoint::postgres`, and `memory::sqlite` — sibling backends in two conventions, one ambiguous about its owning crate. The census output is indistinguishable from real drift.
**Root cause:** A burst that adds rows in a new naming convention fails to normalize pre-existing rows to the same convention. The gate was designed for a uniformly-named corpus; the mid-corpus naming change made it structurally inoperable without any error.
**Proposed fix:** When adding rows that introduce a new naming convention to a registry, normalize ALL existing rows to the new convention in the same burst. Document the convention choice in the registry header. Naming conventions are not optional for mechanically-checked tables.
**Applicable to:** All registry tables with mechanical census commands. Convention changes require full-corpus normalization.

### L-059 [codified]: PROSE CLAIMS OF COMPLETENESS ARE UNFALSIFIABLE

**Discovered:** P1D-172b / OBS-P172b-B (no positive-coverage assertion in any census gate, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** Every criticality census has been a prose claim ("sweep complete", "full module-universe coverage") with no countable artifact. No gate requires a recorded triple (decomposition_tiered_rows, registry_rows, matched_rows). This is why F-P172b-01/02/03/04 all survived — they were unfalsifiable given the information recorded in the changelog.
**Root cause:** Prose completeness claims cannot be independently verified; they rely entirely on the specialist's own completeness check, which can be incorrect (as demonstrated). The adversary has no ground truth to check against except the prose claim itself.
**Proposed fix:** Require every census to emit a coverage triple and gate the burst on the arithmetic: decomposition_tiered_rows − exempt == matched_rows. Record the triple in the burst changelog. Any burst that reports "sweep complete" without a verifiable arithmetic backing is a procedural violation.
**Applicable to:** All registry census operations. Extend to other enumerable artifacts (BC counts, VP counts, gate counts) — those that currently DO have verifiable arithmetic are already protected; this lesson targets the census gates that don't.

### L-060 [codified]: FIXING THE PRIMARY SITE OF A DEFECT WITHOUT SWEEPING DOWNSTREAM PROSE IS THE DOMINANT RECURRENCE PATTERN IN THIS CASCADE

**Discovered:** P1D-172b (multiple findings per same mechanism, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** F-P172b-01/03/04/11/12/13 are each a burst-273/274 fix whose siblings or downstream text were left stale. For example: `module-decomposition.md` v1.26 adjudicated `server::cron` canonical and named "observability.md Module column" as a downstream fix — only the `server::cron` row was fixed; six siblings carrying the identical defect were not. The BC-INDEX preamble was fixed but the footnote in the same section was not. The registry tier was updated but the H2 heading was not. This is the single most common recurrence mechanism across the entire Phase 1d cascade.
**Root cause:** Specialists and orchestrators treat "fix the cited location" as completing the obligation. The TD-VSDD-060 sibling-sweep requirement is understood as applying to symbol names and function signatures but is not consistently applied to prose strings, count literals, heading annotations, and downstream document references.
**Codified fix:** Every defect closure must be followed by a CLASS sweep, not an INSTANCE fix. Before declaring a defect closed: (1) identify every OTHER location where the same defect class could manifest; (2) verify each is either correct or now fixed; (3) record the sweep result in the burst changelog. The sibling-sweep obligation (TD-VSDD-060) applies to ALL artifact types: code, prose, tables, headings, and downstream document references.
**Applicable to:** All fix-bursts. This is a standing discipline, not a one-time fix.

### L-061 [codified]: A SUPPRESSION CLAUSE MUST BE SELF-FALSIFYING

**Discovered:** burst-275 (three-generation defect chain, 2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** One defect class produced three consecutive generations in a single burst: (1) F-P172b-05 gate #25 Part B exemption inverted the check direction; (2) Wave A fix flattened Class A (non-row) and Class B (exempt-row) into one 6-entry list, making `exempt_count` ambiguous and the blocking identity structurally misfiring; (3) Wave B crate-level annotation "no 1:1 decomposition module" asserted an untruth while the Qualifier cell enumerated the three modules that do exist.
**Root cause:** Each "fix" introduced a new suppression clause that caused a downstream check to be skipped. A suppression clause without its own verification obligation becomes the next hiding place for the same defect class.
**Codified fix:** Any clause that causes a gate or check to be skipped must itself carry an explicit verification obligation (a test, an assertion, or a falsifiable condition). "I declare this exempt" without "here is how you verify the exemption is still correct" is a suppression-without-verification and must be rejected at authoring time.
**Applicable to:** All gate authoring and exemption specification. Extends L-053 (exemptions must be annotated) to require the annotation also state the verification criterion.

### L-062 [codified]: CENSUS FIGURES MUST BE COMPUTED SET OPERATIONS, NOT PROSE ASSERTIONS

**Discovered:** burst-275 Wave B reopening #2 (2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** Architect reported `matched_rows = 69` with all validators PASS. Independent set computation by orchestrator returned 66. The difference was a concrete set: {macros::tool, macros::entrypoint, macros::task} — three HIGH-tiered modules surviving the burst that claimed to close their class.
**Root cause:** The census compared a count (69) to an expectation (69) without printing the difference set. A count comparison can be satisfied by compensating errors; a difference set cannot.
**Codified fix:** Census closures must print the difference set inline: `tiered_modules − registry_modules = {empty}`. If the set is empty, print `EMPTY`. If not, every element must be resolved before the burst can close. The sextuple format (decomposition_total_rows, decomposition_tiered_rows, exempt_count, registry_rows, registry_distinct_modules, matched_rows, difference_set) is the canonical census record.
**Applicable to:** All criticality registry censuses. Extends L-059 (require coverage triple) to require the full sextuple including the difference set.

### L-063 [codified]: SPECIALIST VALIDATOR SELF-REPORTS ARE NOT AUTHORITATIVE

**Discovered:** burst-275 Wave A reopening #1 and Wave B reopening #2 (2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** Both waves reported "all validators PASS" while the census identity had failed (Wave B: difference set non-empty) and the gate itself was structurally misfiring (Wave A: ambiguous exempt_count). Validator scripts passing does not imply the logical conditions the validators were written to enforce are actually satisfied — it implies the scripts executed without error.
**Root cause:** The orchestrator accepted specialist self-disclosure of "PASS" without independently verifying the key logical identity. This is the validator equivalent of the "implementer claims MVP scope is sufficient" anti-pattern blocked by TD-VSDD-059.
**Codified fix:** The orchestrator must independently verify the sextuple arithmetic and print the difference set before accepting census closure. This is not a check against the specialist — it is the orchestrator's own closing obligation. Extends TD-VSDD-059 (implementer self-disclosure not authoritative) to all specialist self-reports on enumerable properties.
**Applicable to:** All census-based gate closures. The orchestrator's independent verification is load-bearing, not ceremonial.

### L-064 [codified]: STATE THE COUNTING METHOD WITH ANY COUNT

**Discovered:** burst-275 Wave B reopening #2 (2026-07-26)
**Symptom:** Orchestrator's first recount of tiered modules returned 67 where the truth was 69. A naive pipe-field extraction silently dropped two rows carrying an extra column. The incorrect count looked plausible and was nearly accepted.
**Root cause:** A bare number with no accompanying derivation is not reproducible. Two people counting the same table can get different answers if they use different counting methods; neither number is traceable to a specific extraction procedure.
**Codified fix:** Every count in a census record must travel with its derivation method: the column extracted, the filter applied, whether header rows were excluded, and whether any rows required special handling. "matched_rows = 69" must accompany "counted by: inner join on Module+Qualifier key, excluding header row, including all crate-level annotation rows only when Qualifier cell enumerates distinct modules". The derivation is what makes the count reproducible.
**Applicable to:** All census operations, count records, and enumeration claims across the entire corpus.

| Lesson | Proposed Policy | Scope | Status |
|--------|----------------|-------|--------|
| L-036 | After relocation/rename: sweep JUSTIFICATION prose and INVARIANT SATISFIABILITY narratives, not just symbol occurrences | Relocation checklist | Codified (F-P171a-01, burst-272 relocation residue) |
| L-037 | Canonical-name adjudication must verify the replacement identifier is defined, fielded, placed, and `#[non_exhaustive]` — not just used | Gate #32 new-type checklist | Codified (F-P171a-02, ToolConfig undefined) |
| L-038 | `#[non_exhaustive]` cross-crate relocation requires re-deriving all match-exhaustiveness obligations in BCs, VPs, and CI gates | Relocation checklist | Codified (F-P171a-04, ActionRisk cross-crate) |
| L-039 | Canonical annotation examples must be checked for CORRECT VALUE, not just valid enum membership — wrong values invisibly weaken security gates | Adversary standing probe + spec authoring | Codified (F-P171a-03, BashTool annotation) |
| L-040 | Infrastructure-split passes must record NARROW scope, carry forward unexecuted axes, and must not advance the 3-CLEAN streak | Adversary protocol + orchestrator | Codified (P1D-171 orchestration deviation) |
| L-041 | A rename is not complete until the REPLACEMENT identifier is defined, fielded, module-placed, and anchored — usage-majority adjudication establishes canonical naming only | Gate #32 new-type checklist | Codified (F-P171a-02, ToolConfig undefined, burst-273) |
| L-042 | Adding a module to the decomposition obligates all THREE live criticality-sibling documents in the same burst: module-decomposition.md, module-criticality.md, AND verification-coverage-matrix.md | Criticality-sibling discipline (L-007 extension) | Codified (tools::config propagation gap, burst-273) |
| L-043 | A newly-minted mechanical gate should be expected to find MORE violations than the motivating findings — treat its first full run as a discovery pass, not a coverage confirmation | Factory infrastructure + gate management | Codified (validator #7, burst-273) |
| L-044 | Specialist self-reported sweep completeness is unreliable in BOTH directions — false "zero remaining" and false "complete propagation" claims have both occurred; orchestrator-side independent verification before commit is load-bearing, not ceremonial | Orchestrator protocol | Codified (burst-272 false-zero claim + burst-273 incomplete propagation, burst-273) |
| L-045 | Cross-agent precedence disputes must be adjudicated against the written precedence rules (CLAUDE.md Source-of-Truth Precedence), not by seniority of the asserting agent — the architect's VP-wins-over-BC claim was outside CLAUDE.md rule 4's scope; re-adjudication produced a third and more precise answer | Agent routing + spec governance | Codified (F-P171a-02b VP vs BC precedence, burst-273) |
| L-046 | When a defect class is fixed in one instance, sweep the CLASS, not the instance — gate #25 Part C `awk` field fix (F-P170-15) was never applied to gate #33's structurally identical census, which sat broken for two more passes | Sibling-sweep discipline (TD-VSDD-060) | Codified (F-P172a-01, burst-274) |
| L-047 | A count reduction ("ALL FOUR"→"ALL THREE") is a multi-site edit — six live sites survived inside the very gate whose changelog declared the fix complete; the TD-VSDD-060 sibling-sweep obligation applies to prose strings and count literals as much as to symbol names | Gate authoring + count propagation | Codified (F-P172a-03, burst-274) |
| L-048 | An exemption list must be validated against the architecture docs for EVERY named member — `memory::skills` was granted a definitions-only exemption while `purity-boundary-map.md` §Effectful Shell placement refutes the stated prerequisite; the exemption's own criterion ("no I/O") was not verified against the purity map | Gate #32 exemption authoring | Codified (F-P172a-04, burst-274) |
| L-049 | A census that returns a permanent false failure is worse than no census — it trains operators to ignore the gate; gate #36's `VP-*.md` glob catching `VP-INDEX.md` is a permanent false failure; any census whose "expected: empty" assertion is structurally unsatisfiable must be fixed immediately | Gate quality | Codified (F-P172a-13, burst-274) |
| L-050 | Registry/enumeration lists inside gates (Form-B file set, supplement set, BC count) decay silently; prefer deriving them from the corpus or mechanizing them rather than hardcoding; stale "95 BCs" at six sites all stem from earlier sweeps that updated some but not all instances | Gate maintenance + count propagation | Codified (F-P172a-07/08, burst-274) |
| L-051 | A broken gate hides everything it was built to find — repairing the census commands (gate #33 field-index, gate #25 awk pipeline) immediately exposed 18 missing criticality rows that 172 adversarial passes had not caught, because the gates that would have detected the gap could not execute | Gate quality + enforcement mechanisms | Codified (F-P172a-01 census repair → 18-module discovery, burst-274) |
| L-052 | Backfills scoped by one attribute silently omit everything lacking it — the criticality backfill was scoped to VP-bearing modules, so every execution-logic module without a VP was skipped across all backfill generations, not just D21 | Backfill completeness discipline | Codified (18-module criticality gap, burst-274) |
| L-053 | Intentional absence must be annotated — exempt modules were indistinguishable from forgotten ones, which is why the gap survived 172 passes; every exemption now carries an explicit in-place annotation so the distinction is permanent and machine-readable | Exemption annotation discipline | Codified (core::documents / memory::skills exemptions, burst-274) |
| L-054 | A mechanical gate's short-circuit is a silent coverage hole — both changelog validators checked Form A first and returned early, so Form-B body tables were never validated; four version rows in both-forms files were lost undetected across all prior bursts | Validator implementation discipline | Codified (F-P172a-14 both-forms gap, burst-274) |
| L-055 | "Outside this burst's scope" is not a valid deferral when the defect is the same class in the same files already being edited — the corpus-wide criticality sweep was initially proposed as a separate future burst; that boundary was correctly rejected under the production-grade default | Scope-boundary discipline (production-grade default §Rule 4) | Codified (18-module sweep initially deferred, burst-274) |
| L-056 | Never diff against a derived figure you have not recounted — burst-274's entire sweep was gated on a "56-module universe" that had never equaled the artifact it named (actual 70); the arithmetic was impossible on its face (a 48-row registry cannot yield 18 gaps against a 56-row universe); recount the baseline before trusting the delta | Census baseline discipline | Codified (F-P172b-02, burst-275) |
| L-057 | An exemption clause that narrows the iteration domain can invert the check it guards — the gate #25 Part B exemption added in burst-274 ended "only check modules present in the arch-registry", which suppressed the very absence class the burst was closing; exemptions must name what is exempt, never narrow the iteration universe | Gate #25 exemption authoring | Codified (F-P172b-05, burst-275) |
| L-058 | A census that compares strings requires ONE naming convention — burst-274 added `crate::module` rows to a registry that was ~45% prose-named, leaving ~30 of 66 rows unmatchable; the census output becomes indistinguishable from real drift when conventions are mixed in the same column | Naming-convention discipline | Codified (F-P172b-06, burst-275) |
| L-059 | Prose claims of completeness ("sweep complete", "full coverage") are unfalsifiable without a countable artifact — every criticality census has been a prose claim with no recorded triple; require every census to emit a coverage triple (decomposition_tiered_rows, registry_rows, matched_rows) and gate the burst on the arithmetic | Census completeness discipline | Codified (OBS-P172b-B, burst-275) |
| L-060 | Fixing the primary site of a defect and not sweeping downstream prose is the dominant recurrence pattern in this cascade — F-P172b-01/03/04/11/12/13 are each a burst-273/274 fix whose siblings or downstream text were left stale; every defect-class fix must sweep ALL sites of the same class before declaring closed | Sibling-sweep discipline (TD-VSDD-060 extension) | Codified (multiple findings P1D-172b, 2026-07-26) |
| L-061 | A suppression clause must be self-falsifying — this burst produced three consecutive generations of one defect class, each introduced by the fix for the previous: F-P172b-05 inverted check direction; Wave A fix flattened Class A/B and made exempt_count ambiguous; crate-level annotation asserted an untruth enabling three HIGH-tiered modules to hide; any clause that causes a check to be skipped must itself carry a verification obligation or it becomes the next hiding place | Gate authoring + suppression discipline | Codified (reopening chain burst-275, 2026-07-26) |
| L-062 | Census figures must be computed set operations, not prose assertions — matched_rows was reported as 69 where the computed intersection was 66; the fix is structural: require the difference set be printed inline and be empty; a count can be asserted, a difference set cannot | Census discipline | Codified (Wave B reopening #2, burst-275, 2026-07-26) |
| L-063 | Specialist validator self-reports are not authoritative — both waves reported all validators PASS while the census identity had failed; the orchestrator must independently recompute set operations before accepting closure; this extends TD-VSDD-059 logic beyond the implementer to every specialist | Orchestrator protocol + independent verification | Codified (both reopenings burst-275, 2026-07-26) |
| L-064 | State the counting method with any count — first recount returned 67 tiered where truth was 69 because a naive pipe-field extraction silently dropped two rows carrying an extra column; a bare number is not reproducible; the derivation must travel with the count | Census reproducibility discipline | Codified (Wave B reopening #2, burst-275, 2026-07-26) |
| L-065 | Layer-scoped sweeps leave survivors in the layers they exclude — three independent slices converged on this: every sweep declaring "in this file" / "in architecture-layer docs" / "in domain-spec tree" left survivors in excluded layers; record the sweep predicate, never the layer; a closure statement may not be layer-scoped unless it enumerates excluded layers as named follow-ups | Sweep scope discipline | Codified (three-slice convergence P1D-173, 2026-07-27) |
| L-066 | An existence-checking validator suite reports PASS while semantic defects accumulate — all 9 validators PASS at 130 findings in P1D-173; existence checks cannot substitute for semantic review; three recommended mechanical additions (sub-anchor nesting, label-noun presence, reverse coverage) would have caught 10 of 15 findings in one slice alone | Validator coverage discipline | Codified (P1D-173 process-gap class, 2026-07-27) |
| L-067 | A decision with zero inbound citations is a defect signal — ADR-017 Decision 4 was cited by nothing while four artifacts incorrectly attributed its content to a different ADR; reverse-coverage (flag any accepted-ADR decision with zero inbound citations) is a cheap, high-yield check that would have surfaced this without a human adversary pass | Reverse-coverage discipline | Codified (F-P173-701/702 two-slice convergence P1D-173, 2026-07-27) |
| L-068 | The finding-count plateau was a coverage artifact — ~20 findings per pass for ~45 consecutive passes was re-auditing audited surfaces, not approaching convergence; P1D-173's 130-finding jump came entirely from surfaces never previously read at method granularity; never infer convergence from a finding-rate trend without a coverage ledger stating what has been read at what depth | Coverage ledger discipline | Codified (P1D-173 jump analysis, 2026-07-27) |
| L-069 | Verify a delegate's tool profile before designing a workflow around it — three full-perimeter adversary dispatches were lost because the orchestrator asked a read-only agent to write files; the adversary tool profile is Read/Grep/Glob only (no Write, no Bash); any workflow requiring file output from the adversary must be redesigned before dispatch | Tool-profile verification discipline | Codified (P1D-173 dispatch failures, 2026-07-27) |
| L-070 | Every new validator must be proven against known-positive input before it is trusted — `verify-red-gate-consistency.sh` shipped reporting 0 violations against a corpus with 3; a green board on a corpus with known defects manufactures false confidence and suppresses the class the check was minted to catch; require a demonstrated failure, not a reported count | Validator soundness discipline | Codified (CHECK6 false-neg in-burst, burst-276-wave-A, 2026-07-27) |
| L-071 | Read validator output in full, not by tail — CHECK6's false-neg emitted correct WARN lines above a summary that contradicted them; a tailed read showed only the summary; summaries must be treated as derived, not authoritative; always read the full evidence before trusting the summary count | Validator output reading discipline | Codified (CHECK6 false-neg in-burst, burst-276-wave-A, 2026-07-27) |
| L-072 | Never hardcode a column index adjacent to a mutating table — gate #25 Part C `awk` index broke twice in consecutive bursts from column insertions (F-P170-15 fixed `$4`→`$3`; F-P173-319 broke it again with Qualifier column addition); derive positions from the header at runtime; any hardcoded field index in a gate that touches a growing table is a latent defect | Gate authoring discipline | Codified (F-P173-319 second break, burst-276-wave-A, 2026-07-27) |
| L-073 | Land new checks advisory-first when the defects they detect are still open — a blocking check minted alongside its own unfixed violations blocks the burst that would fix them; advisory + documented promotion path + runtime-computed counts turns each check into a countable target instead of a roadblock; this is not a quality compromise, it is correct sequencing | Advisory-first gate sequencing | Codified (CHECK1/2/3/4/6 design decision, burst-276-wave-A, 2026-07-27) |
| L-074 | Mechanical checks can out-find fresh-context review on mechanizable axes — CHECK1 found 17 sub-anchor violations where the adversary found 4; CHECK4 found 52 non-canonical cells where the adversary found 36; on an axis that can be precisely defined, mechanization is more reliable than re-reviewing; invest in mechanizing axes rather than running additional adversary passes on the same surface | Mechanization-over-review discipline | Codified (CHECK1/CHECK4 baseline vs adversary count, burst-276-wave-A, 2026-07-27) |
| L-075 | Gate #37 (layer-scoped-sweep ban) proved itself on its first burst — a four-site defect was closed only because three successive specialists each swept corpus-wide; each found the next site; layer-scoped sweeps would have closed ADR-010 and declared victory while three sites remained live | Sweep scope discipline (L-065 first real-world application) | Codified (F-P173-211 four-site FerrochainError, burst-276-content-1, 2026-07-27) |
| L-076 | A partial reproduction with no "partial" marker hides defects indefinitely — `api-surface.md` reproduced 4 of `FerrochainError`'s 6 fields for 173 passes; the two omitted fields were exactly where the compile break and the credential-safety constraint lived; either reproduce a definition completely or delegate by pointer, never silently abridge | Reproduction completeness discipline | Codified (F-P173-202/211 api-surface abridgement, burst-276-content-1, 2026-07-27) |
| L-077 | A count matching is not the same as the right row being captured — the canonicality filter fix was verified by proving the specific dropped row (`eval::judge`, sole occupant of the excluded H3) is now in the extracted set, not by observing the total reached 71; a count can be asserted, a set membership cannot | Census verification discipline | Codified (canonicality filter 70→71, burst-276-content-1, 2026-07-27) |
| L-078 | Inline the reference material for short, surgical dispatches — two attempts at the `entities-server.md` single-line edit died during reference reads; inlining ADR-010's canonical struct and the casing canon into the prompt completed it in ~100 seconds with 6 tool calls; long prompts with complete context are cheaper than short prompts that must read during execution | Dispatch efficiency discipline | Codified (entities-server single-edit failures, burst-276-content-1, 2026-07-27) |
| L-079 | A phantom identifier propagates outward from a single wrong declaration; deleting the source fixes nothing downstream — `PathGuard::check` was invented by one interface declaration and reached 16 sites across 6 files, outnumbering the real name in the BC layer; every phantom-symbol fix must be paired with a corpus-wide sweep of the invented name, not just removal of its origin | Phantom-sweep discipline | Codified (F-P173-601 16-site PathGuard::check sweep, burst-276-content-2, 2026-07-27) |
| L-080 | Hand counts in this corpus run systematically low — third instance in one day: adversary 4 vs CHECK1's 17; adversary 36 vs CHECK4's 52; specialist 10 vs actual 16 on this sweep; direction is consistent, not random — treat any hand-asserted count as a lower bound until a script confirms it | Count verification discipline | Codified (PathGuard::check 10-estimated vs 16-actual sweep, burst-276-content-2, 2026-07-27) |
| L-081 | A bare ID-range anchor asserts coverage that per-method precision disproves — "BC-2.08.001 through BC-2.08.005" concealed three signature defects and one entirely unanchored BC; range anchors are unfalsifiable at member granularity; require per-member anchoring wherever a range spans distinct obligations | Per-method anchor discipline | Codified (F-P173-614 §Runnable and §BaseChatModel range anchor removal, burst-276-content-2, 2026-07-27) |
| L-082 | A PASS on a corpus with known defects manufactures false confidence — every validator must explicitly name the defect families it cannot detect before its PASS output can be trusted; in this burst, vacuous `version-gt-1.0` guard passing on absent `version:` fields, silent skips of Form-B body tables, `non-standard-rev-format` WARNs masking unverified direction, and a tautological blocking census identity each returned PASS or WARN while material defects remained; treat any validator's PASS as "defects in my named blind spots may still be present" | Validator false-confidence discipline | Codified (F-B276-02 dominant defect family of this pass, burst-276-content-3, 2026-07-27) |
| L-083 | Blocking validators not consulted before commit means regressions get committed — both prior burst sessions introduced blocking failures that were caught only after the commit: burst-276-content-1 introduced TD-VSDD-091 live-body version pins with `verify-no-version-pins` failing; burst-276-signatures inserted out-of-order changelog entries with `verify-form-a-changelog-direction` failing; the validators worked correctly — nothing ran them pre-commit; the full blocking validator suite MUST run before declaring a fix-burst commit-ready | Pre-commit validator discipline | Codified (committed regression gap, burst-276-content-3, 2026-07-27) |
| L-084 | Bare ID references to ephemeral context are unactionable — approximately 60 P1D-173 findings exist as bare IDs only because the adversarial pass report pointed to "orchestrator dispatch notes" that were never committed to a file; those notes lived in a prior session's context and are unrecoverable; every adversarial pass report MUST persist per-finding detail (severity, affected artifact, routing) as committed prose; a finding that references context-only notes cannot be routed, cannot be fixed, and cannot be verified closed | Adversarial pass record completeness | Codified (unrecoverable findings gap, burst-276-content-3, 2026-07-27) |
| L-085 | Changelog direction must be observed from the file, not asserted by the dispatcher — the orchestrator told three product-owner dispatches that `prd-supplements/` files use ASCENDING order; the correct direction is DESCENDING; `behavioral-contracts/` is ASCENDING; direction has been asserted wrong twice by the orchestrator in this session, causing blocking regressions; the reliable method is to read the file's existing changelog tail and let the specialist observe the direction directly rather than trusting an asserted rule | Changelog direction discipline | Codified (orchestrator direction regression, burst-276-content-3, 2026-07-27) |
| L-086 | A mitigation placed after the hazard it guards against offers protection only to readers who finish the document — ADR-007 §Full Crate Roster placed the 21-crate forward amendment after a heading, a blockquote labeled "Authoritative.", and a total line, all asserting 18 crates; a reader consuming the document sequentially could commit to the wrong count and never reach the correction; any disclaimer or amendment that operates only when fully read must precede the content it guards, not follow it | Mitigation placement discipline | Codified (F-B276-01 reading-position trap, burst-276-content-3, 2026-07-27) |
| L-087 | Gates scoped by label are blind to dialect variants — `verify-form-a-changelog-direction` PASSes 6 files with no `changelog:` key; gate #25 keys on `ROLL-UP` (0 occurrences) while the actual vocabulary is `crate-level — …`; the F-P96-01 sweep normalized to `Module` and was blind to `Architecture Module`; every gate that keys on a label must enumerate all dialect forms of that label, or it must scope by value pattern | Gate dialect-blindness discipline | Codified (P1D-174 structural finding, six independent sites, 2026-07-27) |
| L-088 | A validator must report UNVERIFIED, never PASS, for inputs it structurally cannot evaluate — `verify-form-a-changelog-direction` reported `UNVERIFIED=0` while emitting vacuous PASS for 6 files lacking the key it validates; a validator that cannot determine the truth value of a predicate must emit UNVERIFIED and count it; conflating "no key → no violation" with "key examined → PASS" manufactures false confidence of exactly the class this burst was supposed to eliminate | Validator false-confidence discipline (PASS-on-unverifiable) | Codified (F-ORCH-174-04 / CRIT 9, P1D-174, 2026-07-27) |
| L-089 | Changelog claims are not evidence of closure — six false closures were found in a single pass, two of them CRIT; a changelog entry can be authored in the same burst that fails to apply the change; every closure requires a body diff confirming the stated change is present; "v1.2 changelog claims X" is not the same as "X is in the v1.2 body" | False-closure discipline | Codified (6 false closures in P1D-174, 2026-07-27) |
| L-090 | Read-only agents cannot persist findings — record adversarial passes immediately or lose the work; 5 of 13 P1D-174 slices died on transient API `Connection closed mid-response` at larger dispatch sizes; their work was unrecoverable because the adversary tool profile is Read/Grep/Glob only; slices must be sized to complete inside the API timeout window (~40 BC files or ~2 dense supplement files max); the orchestrator must record the pass as a state-manager burst before dispatching any fix agents | Adversarial pass record immediacy | Codified (D-40, 5 lost slices P1D-174, 2026-07-27) |
| L-091 | Defect density is inversely correlated with file version number — the densest findings in P1D-174 came from BC files at v1.0/v1.1/v1.2 (BC-2.21.001, BC-2.19.002, BC-2.20.001, BC-2.20.003); prior passes concentrated on frequently-amended files and left the low-version files un-audited; a coverage ledger must track read depth per file, not just per-pass finding counts | Coverage ledger by file depth | Codified (second structural finding P1D-174, 2026-07-27) |
| L-092 | Existence-only citation validation cannot catch semantic mis-anchors or type incoherence — `api-surface.md` v1.10 quoted an E0038-invalid `dyn Tool` line while fixing the same row's subsystem anchor metadata; the anchor validator passed; a row can have the right anchor text and a fatally wrong type signature; semantic and structural checks are orthogonal to existence checks | Semantic vs existence validation discipline | Codified (F-P174-801/615 dyn-incompatibility, P1D-174, 2026-07-27) |
| L-093 | A security control specified as "best-effort" or "documented as expected behavior (not a bug)" is an unimplemented control — rate-limit bypass via LRU eviction was blessed in spec prose as expected behavior while being a repeatable full quota reset for any attacker; MCP error sanitization was marked "best-effort in v1" with no predicate; controls that cannot be specified to a verifiable predicate must be removed from the spec, not left as unfalsifiable caveats | Security control completeness discipline | Codified (SEC-1 F-P174-861, SEC-2 F-P174-814, P1D-174, 2026-07-27) |
| L-094 | Gate provenance: every validator assertion must be grounded in a ratified rule citable in bc-authoring-plan.md or a template before it ships; verify-bc-frontmatter-schema asserted three invented requirements (required `id:` key, universal `red_gate:`, universal `vp_seed:`) producing 336 of 342 findings as false positives and nearly triggering a 129-file product-owner churn burst against a correctly-authored corpus; the source of each check must be a cited gate number or template section, not inferred convention | Gate provenance discipline | Codified (D-46 gate-provenance, fix-burst 277 Wave A follow-up, 2026-07-28) |
| L-095 | Delete ungrounded checks, never downgrade to advisory — an advisory that cannot be grounded in a ratified rule trains readers to ignore the gate's output and silently accumulates the defect class it was minted to catch; verify-adr-decision-refs Check 4 attempted semantic ADR review that its own header classified as the adversary's job; deleted, not downgraded; advisory status is for sound checks whose findings are non-blocking, not for unsound checks | Advisory vs delete discipline | Codified (D-46 gate-provenance, fix-burst 277 Wave A follow-up, 2026-07-28) |
| L-096 | Validate values, not labels — three independent instances in one burst all keyed on a label string rather than the value the label named: (a) verify-bc-frontmatter-schema validated label presence rather than field values; (b) verify-form-a-changelog-direction carried a label-based carve-out ("v1.0 exception does not apply to BCs") found nowhere in gate #28; (c) verify-adr-decision-refs Check 4 validated decision-label presence instead of semantic decision content; every gate must state the value predicate being tested, never just the label it keys on | Value-vs-label discipline | Codified (D-46 gate-provenance, fix-burst 277 Wave A follow-up, 2026-07-28) |
| L-097 | Enforcement-layer gates need their own independent audit — pre-commit-validators.sh claimed to run "all BLOCKING validators" while silently omitting verify-changelog-date-monotonicity and verify-enum-variant-casing (both exit 1 on FAIL); a runner that under-reports its own coverage certifies an unmeasured state with exactly the same false confidence the P1D-174 CRIT 9 finding family targeted; the runner itself must be audited against the blocking-validator roster, not just copied | Pre-commit runner audit discipline | Codified (fix-burst 277 Wave A follow-up runner gap, 2026-07-28) |
| L-098 | A single-form grep cannot verify a multi-form claim — Wave D's v1.13 "zero additional hits" claim was false because the author searched one string form and missed a structurally different label (`**PO BC obligations:**` vs the searched form) used at 5 instances across 3 shards; a closure statement must quote the exact searched terms and enumerate structurally distinct alternative forms, or it cannot be machine-verified; the correction entries now quote the searched terms | Grep-completeness discipline | Codified (Wave D root cause, fix-burst 277 Wave D, 2026-07-28) |
| L-099 | Declaring a specialist's work done without running the gate that covers the files just edited is the dominant false-green recurrence in this cascade — Wave B declared DONE while verify-no-version-pins was FAILING (PASS=193 FAIL=5) on eleven volatile ADR-NNN vN.N body pins introduced by the wave's own edits, and reported only 3 validators while omitting the failing one; caught only by orchestrator independent verification; every specialist declaration of done must be accompanied by a full blocking-validator run on the touched surface | Specialist pre-declare validator discipline | Codified (orchestrator self-defect 1, fix-burst 277 Wave B, 2026-07-28) |
| L-100 | Pattern-probe coverage must never be credited as file coverage — 19 HIGH-or-above findings in P1D-175 were invisible to shape-matching; a probe that checks for shape-conformance cannot substitute for a line-by-line body read; coverage credit for a document requires reading its actual content at the depth the gate claims to audit | Coverage-depth discipline | Codified (P1D-175 coverage-debt structure, 2026-07-28) |
| L-101 | Perimeter construction must be Glob-derived and differenced against the slice manifest — a document's absence from every slice is a hard dispatch error, not a permissible outcome; construct the perimeter list, assign each document to exactly one slice, verify the difference set is empty before dispatch | Perimeter-integrity discipline | Codified (P1D-175 slice-split recovery, 2026-07-28) |
| L-102 | A fix-burst's own attestations are its least reliable artifacts — three false-closure attestations were minted inside commit 2d36282 (BC-2.20.001, BC-2.20.002, BC-2.21.002, BC-2.22.001 modified with no version bump, no changelog, no BC-INDEX entry); the commit author cannot audit their own output; every burst commit must be followed by a reviewer verification step, not just an attestation | Fix-burst self-attestation discipline | Codified (D221 confirmed against fix-burst 277, P1D-175, 2026-07-28) |
| L-103 | Sibling sweeps scoped to "in this file" systematically miss — a sweep-boundary manifest specifying the exact grep predicate and the exact file set searched must travel with every closure statement; "in this file" is not a sweep predicate; it lets survivors hide in every file not explicitly named | Sweep-boundary manifest discipline | Codified (P1D-175 sweep-boundary pattern, 2026-07-28) |
| L-104 | Canonicalization must run source-first, not consumer-first — the `as_retriever` adjudication mutated 4 documents and 11+ sites before dyn-compatibility was compile-tested; canonical type decisions must be validated at the source (compile the Rust signature in a scratch context) before propagating to consumer documents; propagation that precedes validation inverts the dependency | Source-first canonicalization discipline | Codified (D-48 D-45 overturn, P1D-175, 2026-07-28) |
| L-105 | A guard against false success can itself contain a false-success path — publish-all.sh v0 reported SUCCESS when all names were held by a squatter; the fix introduced SQUATTED/UNKNOWN hard-fail classifications and a self-test fixture that covers all four outcomes (AVAILABLE/OWNED/SQUATTED/UNKNOWN) and must exit 1 in the absence of the SUCCESS line; any guard that cannot self-demonstrate failure on known-bad input is false-green | Guard self-falsification discipline | Codified (publish-all.sh 3-way classification, 2026-07-28) |
| L-106 | Independent verification of adversary claims is load-bearing — slice D2's severity for `as_retriever` was correct (dyn-incompatible) but its cited error code was wrong (E0307 instead of E0038); acting on the error code alone would have fixed a non-existent error and left the real one live; when a claim drives a structural adjudication, independently verify it with a fresh compile or source read before acting | Adversary-claim verification discipline | Codified (D-48 compile-test overturning D-45 &Arc<Self>, 2026-07-28) |
| L-107 | VP harnesses must assert against production code, never their own test doubles — a harness that constructs a test double to verify properties of the test double proves nothing about the production implementation; every VP proof and fuzz target must exercise the actual production function; a harness detached from production code is a false confidence generator identical in harm to a validator that passes on a corpus with known defects | VP harness-production-binding discipline | Codified (P1D-175 VP-harness coverage debt, 2026-07-28) |
| L-108 | A gate's own artifacts (tests, documentation, remediation records, probe fixtures) will trip the gate unless explicitly excluded from its corpus scope — three gates fired on their own scaffolding in one burst: changelog text quoting the banned signature triggered verify-signature-canon, `bc-authoring-plan.md` grep-command examples matched the pattern they teach, and `records-lint.sh` probe fixtures contained the version pins it detects; every gate file must carry an explicit self-exclusion pathspec before shipping | Gate self-scope exclusion discipline | Codified (D-56, fix-burst 278, 2026-07-28) |
| L-109 | An authoring rule cannot require quoting a term that a gate forbids — requiring changelog claims to quote the searched term is self-defeating because the changelog documenting the fix trips the exact gate whose finding the entry was recording; the fix is to exempt the descriptive register (changelog, remediation prose) from the gate, not to weaken the authoring rule; D-55 records this explicitly so a future agent does not attempt to "harmonize" the two rules | Authoring-rule / forbidden-term discipline | Codified (D-55, fix-burst 278, 2026-07-28) |
| L-110 | Gate-change acceptance is catching a known-live violation, not suite-green — the L9b bug-fix applied a changelog exemption to a version-pin rule that coincidentally covered the `ADR-005 vN.N` pins the architect had just removed; the suite went green because the rule shrank to fit the cleanup; every new exemption must ship with a narrow proof showing the pattern fires in normative position and does NOT fire in the exempt position in the same fixture | Gate-change acceptance discipline | Codified (D-57, fix-burst 278, 2026-07-28) |
| L-111 | A probe fixture must sit in the same semantic register as the real violations it tests — a fixture that exercises a changelog cite tests the rule in the descriptive-register exemption path, not in normative text; a false green from a fixture in the wrong register is indistinguishable from a correct pass and will suppress the defect class the gate was minted to catch until the next adversary pass | Probe-fixture register discipline | Codified (D-57 corollary, fix-burst 278, 2026-07-28) |
| L-112 | Flagging diff deletions as violations inverts the incentive to fix — a gate that fires on removed lines penalises cleanup and rewards leaving the violation in place; gates must scope to additions or current-file state, never to deleted lines; the verify-signature-canon gate learned this when the L9b diff-based approach fired on the removal of an ADR-005 version pin | Diff-deletion inversion discipline | Codified (fix-burst 278 L9b scoping, 2026-07-28) |
| L-113 | "Appears in a diff" is not equivalent to "newly authored" — git's hunk algorithm marks lines as added when surrounding content shifts even if the text itself pre-dates the authoring boundary; the L9b version-pin rule must be grounded in the ratification date of the CONTENT, not the position of the line in the diff; content that pre-dates 2026-07-24 remains grandfathered in changelogs even if the diff shows it as an addition | Diff-presence vs newly-authored discipline | Codified (fix-burst 278 L9b boundary, 2026-07-28) |
| L-114 | Any pattern that legitimately appears on the SOURCE SIDE of a migration arrow must be classified as hazard-naming prose before being flagged as a violation — replacing `Arc<dyn ferrochain_core::Tool>` in the "change THIS to Arc<dyn DynTool>" directive would make the spec say "change DynTool to DynTool"; the correct fix is to add an explicit `(non-object-safe, E0038)` qualifier so the exemption applies for a stated true reason, not silently | Classify-before-replace discipline | Codified (ADR-005 §Wave C S4 annotation, fix-burst 278, 2026-07-28) |
| L-115 | A canonicalization sweep must fix SOURCE documents first and DERIVED documents second — running the sweep in reverse order makes the derived documents' state canonical and the sources stale, which is exactly what happened when all 13 VP files had their consumer-side `module:` frontmatter corrected while the registry entry in the architecture source retained the hyphenated form; source-first order is non-negotiable | Canonicalization-sweep source-first discipline | Codified (VP-001..013 module: frontmatter sweep, fix-burst 278, 2026-07-28) |
| L-116 | A routing spec instruction can be wrong about corpus state — the wave-b routing spec instructed "mint E-MEMORY-004" without checking whether that error code already existed; it did exist (under `VAL`/BC-2.15.002); the executing agent must verify corpus state before acting on any "mint new" or "add new entry" instruction; the correct action was recategorization (`VAL`→`SECURITY`), not a duplicate mint | Routing-spec corpus-verification discipline | Codified (D-66 E-MEMORY-004 recategorization, fix-burst 279, 2026-07-28) |
| L-117 | A guard extension must reach the formal harness or the proof cannot see it — VP-006's Kani harness was extended to all three `TemplateInput` arms precisely because the few-shot path had a guard in prose (BC-2.18.003 PC5) but no formal proof coverage; a behavioral contract postcondition without a corresponding harness exercise is untestable at the formal layer; adding the guard and the harness must be one atomic step | Guard-harness coverage discipline | Codified (VP-006 §Harness extended to TemplateInput arms, fix-burst 279, 2026-07-28) |
| L-118 | An enum whose declaration order is the inverse of its security severity is a latent fail-open — `TrustLevel`'s `derive(Ord)` would yield `Trusted` as the aggregate maximum for a slot containing untrusted content because declaration order (`Untrusted`, `UserInput`, `Trusted`) is exactly the wrong ordering for a `.max()` aggregation; prefer an explicit `severity() -> u8` ordinal over declaration order for any type where "highest" has a security-critical meaning; prohibit `Ord::max()` on the type | Enum-ordering security discipline | Codified (D-65 `TrustLevel::severity()`, fix-burst 279, 2026-07-28) |
| L-119 | A security control that fails closed only by convention must be recorded as such — `PromptTemplate::format` has no injection guard by design; this is a convention-enforced boundary, not a type-enforced one; the spec must name which boundaries are type-enforced vs convention-enforced (INV-5, ADR-015) so auditors can distinguish intentional gaps from defects; an undocumented convention-enforced boundary is indistinguishable from an oversight | Convention-vs-type-enforced boundary discipline | Codified (D-63 `PromptTemplate::format` unguarded INV-5, fix-burst 279, 2026-07-28) |
| L-120 | Fixing half a two-part finding leaves the other half live — B202 initially covered only the `MessageListVar` path (first half); the `FewShotExamples` path (second half) remained unguarded because few-shot rendering routes through the explicitly unguarded surface in D-63; the complete fix required both BC-2.18.003 PC5 and BC-2.18.004 PC5 plus extending VP-006 to all three arms; a finding description mentioning multiple input paths is a signal that all paths must be closed in the same burst | Two-part-finding completeness discipline | Codified (B202 `FewShotExamples` path, fix-burst 279, 2026-07-28) |
| L-121 | A breaking signature change requires an enumerated per-owner sweep, not a per-file sweep — changing `format_messages` from `HashMap<String, TemplateVar>` to `HashMap<String, TemplateInput>` touches every callsite in every crate that constructs inputs; the sweep obligation is per callsite-owner (each BC whose acceptance criterion constructs such a map), not per file; missing any owner's BC leaves an infeasible acceptance criterion; enumerate the owner list before declaring the sweep complete | Breaking-signature per-owner sweep discipline | Codified (D-64 `HashMap<String, TemplateInput>` signature change, fix-burst 279, 2026-07-28) |
| L-122 | When editing any Historical Content row in STATE.md, de-pin the ENTIRE row pre-emptively before authoring the edit — records-lint L9b scans all `+` lines in the diff; a pre-existing version pin in an edited row is re-exposed as a newly-authored addition and blocks the commit; cost: three commit attempts in practice; fix: de-pin the full row content in the same edit that makes the substantive change | STATE.md version-pin management discipline | Codified (session wrap D-70, 2026-07-28) |
| L-123 | A sweep claim is falsifiable only if it states the grep pattern used AND the matched file count — product-owner made three successive sweep claims in one burst without stating search terms: phantom BC-2.23.x absence (all 6 exist), phantom ferrochain-macros absence (already in §Constraints), and BC-body sweep of 11 of 53 files declared complete; the correct discipline is to quote the exact grep command and file count so any reader can verify the claim independently, and a sweep completion claim that could not have caught survivors is not a completion claim `[process-gap]` | Corpus-sweep falsifiability discipline | Codified (A25 partial, NF-280-B-01/B-02 retractions, fix-burst 280, 2026-07-28) |
| L-124 | An assertion fix that passes for the same defect it targets is not a fix — architect replaced `is_some()` with a `format!("{:?}")` prefix match that still passed for the bare-string cause the finding named, and justified it with a factually false claim that `downcast_ref` was unavailable; a correct assertion fix must FAIL under the exact defect it targets; `Error::source()` already returns `dyn Error + 'static`, so `downcast_ref::<McpError>()` was available, making `matches!(src.downcast_ref::<McpError>(), Some(McpError::ToolExecution(_)))` the correct fix `[process-gap]` | Assertion-correctness discipline | Codified (A10 VP-004 fix, fix-burst 280, 2026-07-28) |
| L-125 | Paraphrasing a finding from memory instead of quoting its text verbatim produces substitute content that invents false blockers — product-owner invented substitute content for two HIGH findings while declaring them blocked, which would have recorded two false blockers and left two genuine HIGHs open; quote finding text verbatim from the pass report; a blocker claim must cite the specific external dependency that makes the finding unresolvable in current scope, not a paraphrase of the finding that obscures what was actually asked `[process-gap]` | Finding-fidelity discipline | Codified (C207/C208 paraphrase blockers, fix-burst 280, 2026-07-28) |
| L-126 | A count fix that updates only one site while a sibling retains the stale value, then attaches a changelog entry claiming a full sweep, is a recurrence of the C206 class — L2-INDEX simultaneously asserted both 8 and 9 risks in the same document; the correct discipline: grep for the old count with anchored context (e.g., "N risks", "total N") across ALL index files and changelogs before declaring the sweep complete; a count fix is complete only when zero occurrences of the old count remain in any file that claims to state that count `[process-gap]` | Count-propagation discipline | Codified (C206 sibling recurrence in L2-INDEX, fix-burst 280, 2026-07-28) |
| L-127 | A symbol that exists only in a VP body and its verification harness makes a test harness the sole signature authority for production code — `validate_embedding_batch` had no entry in interface-definitions, api-surface, or module-decomposition until this burst; any symbol first appearing in a VP file must be registered in the canonical interface registry in the same commit; a VP harness that invents a function signature it has never seen in an interface spec is a signature-authority inversion, recurrence of the F-P175-B117 class | Production-symbol registration discipline | Codified (A24/A26 VP-008 harness, fix-burst 280, 2026-07-28) |
| L-128 | A deferral framed as "outside scope" when the stale values are in the very table the agent already has open is a scope-avoidance pattern — business-analyst reported Size-band line-count cells were "outside scope" while the document containing them was already open and the correct band values were derivable from the bands defined in the same table; defer only work that genuinely requires a different agent or an unresolved external dependency, not work that is inconvenient to do while the file is already loaded `[process-gap]` | Scope-avoidance discipline | Codified (L2-INDEX band-count deferral, fix-burst 280, 2026-07-28) |
| L-129 | When concurrent agents write to the same files, a reader agent may observe a mid-write state and report defects that do not exist in any stable snapshot — business-analyst reported a records-lint FAIL=1 in BC-INDEX.md that three independent later runs could not reproduce, because the read occurred while a concurrent writer was mid-edit; the mitigation: re-run all validators only after all concurrent writers have quiesced and committed their changes; a validation result taken from a file that is simultaneously being written is untrustworthy regardless of the result code `[process-gap]` | Concurrent-write discipline | Codified (NF-280-B-02 ghost finding, fix-burst 280, 2026-07-28) |
| L-130 | An intentional divergence from the upstream template that is not recorded in a governance register is indistinguishable from a defect — the `Lines` column replacing the template-prescribed `Tokens` column in L2-INDEX had no TDIV governance record, so it read as structural drift and was reported as such; register template divergences at the time of creation, not retroactively when an adversary finds them; an undocumented divergence is always a legitimate finding even when intentional, because without a record the intent cannot be verified `[process-gap]` | Template-divergence governance discipline | Codified (TDIV-001 L2-INDEX Lines-vs-Tokens, fix-burst 280, 2026-07-28) |
| L-131 | A scope-fenced dispatch must explicitly enumerate what it found but was not permitted to touch — a fenced dispatch reported "active-spec sites fully swept" from a census covering only in-scope surfaces and reconciled against an estimate using that same truncated census; the out-of-scope residue was never listed; the architect correctly enumerated its 5 domain-spec sites as out-of-scope; recurrence of the D-57 class: a sweep completion claim is unfalsifiable when the fence is described but the fence-excluded residue is not `[process-gap]` | Scope-fence residue enumeration discipline | Codified (D-35 xtask rename sweep partial, burst-281 Wave A-corr, 2026-07-29) |
| L-132 | Self-test bars must be stated as reconciliation targets, not floors — "correctly classify at least 2 multi-line sites" was satisfied by a detector finding 5 of 48 spans; a floor is satisfiable by a broken implementation (any two correct answers pass it); a reconciliation target ("must account for all 48 spans") is not; a self-test bar that can be met while leaving 43 of 48 instances undetected is not a self-test | Self-test reconciliation-target discipline | Codified (discriminator self-test bar Wave A-corr, burst-281, 2026-07-29) |
| L-133 | A validator that flags its own specification is not done — declaring 10 self-flags in the canon "expected and correct" would have shipped a CI gate that can never be green and a count reproducible only with tribal knowledge; the correct fix is a region exclusion that makes the canon report zero violations on itself, verified by a self-probe in the same fixture; accepted-noise annotations are a paper fix that converts a defect into a governance liability | Validator self-flagging discipline | Codified (ADR-010 canon self-flagging, discriminator defect 2, burst-281 Wave A-corr, 2026-07-29) |
| L-134 | Count-delta claims must be decomposed by effect, never netted — a net move from 144/158 to 170 concealed that the headline defect (multiline span blindspot) contributed 0 to the change and an unnamed fourth defect (`grep -v` false negative on a single file) contributed the entire delta; requiring separate effect attribution (Effect A = 0, Effect B = +1) surfaced Defect 4; a netting that eliminates the per-defect ledger is unfalsifiable | Count-delta decomposition discipline | Codified (170 authoritative count, four-defect decomposition, burst-281 Wave A-corr, 2026-07-29) |
| L-135 | An orchestrator dispatch asserting a corpus convention must cite the validator that enforces it, or omit the assertion and let the specialist read the file — the orchestrator instructed the architect to write changelog entries "ascending order, newest last"; the corpus convention enforced by `verify-form-a-changelog-direction` is descending (newest first); the architect correctly followed the file over the uncited dispatch instruction; an uncited convention claim is as likely to be wrong as the agent's default, and the agent will follow the file anyway | Orchestrator convention-citation discipline | Codified (changelog-direction dispatch error, burst-281 Wave A-corr self-attributed, 2026-07-29) |
| L-136 | Every census must come from a validator with partitioned buckets before trusting the count — the Wave B ADR-010 violation count passed through four different detectors (144, 158, 170, 172) before the executable validator with bucket decomposition (CLASS3_VALID/CLASS2_VALID/CLASS3_VALID_COMPLETE/exclusions) produced a trustworthy figure; a count without a bucket breakdown cannot be independently verified; mint the executable validator and confirm all buckets sum to an independently measured total before acting on any corpus census | Corpus-census discipline | Codified (D-83 verify-error-notation-canon.sh minting, burst-282 Wave B, 2026-07-29) |
| L-137 | Dispatch instructions must supply verification methods, not conclusions — four independent specialist batches in Wave B all derived the correct notation convention when given "observe the file, here is the enforcing validator"; when the dispatch instead asserted "changelogs are descending" (D-87 self-attributed defect 1), it was wrong; supplying the verification method (cite the validator, cite the file) produces more reliable outcomes than supplying the conclusion because the specialist can read the file and the orchestrator's convention assertion is as likely to be stale as the specialist's default | Dispatch-verification-method discipline | Codified (D-87 defect 1, D-85 §class-dependent changelog conventions, burst-282 Wave B, 2026-07-29) |
| L-138 | Concurrent batches cross-check each other on shared gates — a sibling batch running verify-form-a-changelog-direction corpus-wide detected the FAIL=7 introduced by the very first Wave B batch (D-87 defect 2) before the second batch could propagate the same error to additional files; concurrent batches are a defect-detection mechanism as well as a throughput mechanism; dispatching all batches simultaneously and requiring each to run the full blocking-validator suite turns the fan-out into an automatic cross-check | Concurrent-batch cross-check discipline | Codified (D-87 defect 2 detection by sibling batch, burst-282 Wave B, 2026-07-29) |
| L-139 | Pilot before fan-out on any convention sweep — the SS-08 pilot batch (B1) surfaced two canon gaps (D-85 §class-dependent changelog conventions; validator self-flag class) before the full 7-batch fan-out; both gaps, unresolved at fan-out time, would have caused systematic errors across all 51 BC files; cost of pilot: 1 batch + 1 rejection cycle; cost avoided: 7 × N correction cycles; any convention sweep must run a single pilot file and confirm the validator exits zero before committing to the full fan-out | Pilot-before-fan-out discipline | Codified (B1 SS-08 pilot, burst-282 Wave B, 2026-07-29) |
| L-182 | **[PROCESS-GAP / SESSION-MANAGEMENT]** Orphaned prior-session background agents survive into new sessions and can collide with pipeline writers. During the session resume for this burst (P1D-193 convergence recording), a background agent dispatched in the PRIOR session ("Wrap burst-297") survived into the new session: it had staged stale STATE.md content and attempted a git restore of a burst-298 file already committed. Forensic verification confirmed NO committed-state damage — the fix that closed P1D-188/189 survived intact. ROOT CAUSE: the resume procedure's `factory-worktree-health` check does not enumerate or quiesce stale prior-session background agents before dispatching new writers. A new writer that reads STATE.md and a stale orphan that has STATE.md staged with old content are a read-modify-write race condition. REMEDIATION TRIGGER: session-reviewer + engine-vendor at Phase-1-gate close / first self-improvement wave. Because Phase 2 story decomposition has not run (no self-improvement epic exists), this cannot be attached to a follow-up story yet. Tag: `session-management`, `orphaned-agent`, `race-condition`, `resume-procedure`. | Resume procedure safety — pre-dispatch orphan quiesce | Captured as D-168 / Drift table entry; target: session-reviewer + engine-vendor at Phase-1-gate close |
| L-183 | Parallel-authoring error-code race — when two specialists author the same BC-family in parallel (business-analyst authors CAP+DI, product-owner authors BC bodies referencing those error codes), the BA may use blueprint placeholders (E-CORE-NNN/MMM) while the PO mints concrete codes (E-CORE-009/010); this creates integration-drift across CAP/DI/UL vs BC that the atomic burst commit does not catch until an adversary re-pass; the fix: orchestrator pre-assigns concrete error-code IDs in the dispatch prompt before parallel fan-out, or runs a reconciliation grep (`rg 'E-CORE-NNN\|E-CORE-MMM'` across the new files) before committing the atomic scope-burst | Pre-assign error codes before parallel authoring fan-out (or run a reconciliation grep before commit) | Codified (F-P194-02 burst-303/D-172, 2026-08-17) |
| L-184 | **[PROCESS-GAP / SWEEP-DISCIPLINE]** Canonical-form fix-bursts MUST run a corpus-wide grep of the changed token across ALL of `.factory/specs/` BEFORE declaring closure — NOT per-file sweep only. P1D-195 found 6 findings all in the architecture-decision layer (ADR-005, ADR-026) and cross-cutting reference docs (verification-architecture, error-taxonomy) that burst-303's per-file sweep missed: burst-303 correctly fixed `invoke_dyn→invoke`, `core::runnable::parallel→core::runnable`, and `E-CORE-NNN/MMM→E-CORE-009/010` in the specialist-owned files (BC bodies, CAP, DI, UL), but did NOT sweep the ADRs that REFERENCE those same tokens or the cross-cutting verification docs that REPLICATE them. The systematically-missed sibling sites are: (1) the architecture-decision layer (ADRs that document the change in §Consequences, §Decision, §Context, §VP-Recommendation); (2) cross-cutting reference docs that mirror canonical-form tokens (verification-architecture, verification-coverage-matrix, error-taxonomy). REMEDIATION: every fix-burst that changes a canonical-form token MUST conclude with a 4-pattern corpus-wide grep gate before staging: `grep -r "old_token" .factory/specs/` with results reviewed against the legitimate-context exclusion list (changelog rows, legitimate DynTool context). Mechanical enforcement candidate: factory commit hook scanning for residual unresolved placeholder tokens (DEFER-004). Tag: `corpus-sweep`, `architecture-layer-siblings`, `session-reviewer`. | Corpus-wide grep gate on canonical-form changes — architecture-layer ADRs and cross-cutting docs are systematically-missed sibling sites | Codified (OBS-A P1D-195 / burst-304 / D-173, 2026-08-18) |
| L-186 | **[PROCESS-GAP / SWEEP-DISCIPLINE]** AXIS-CARRIER propagation discipline: Adding ANY new taxonomy element (error category, component, DI, CAP, error code, VP) requires sweeping ALL of its canonical-definition sites AND every "N <element>s" / "N-<element> axis" assertion corpus-wide — not just the primary registry.
