For error CATEGORY specifically the carrier set is: (1) error-taxonomy.md §Error Categories table — primary definition; (2) ADR-010 §Category Axis Expansion — enum list + count statement + axis section; (3) BC-2.14.001 — the contract's category enumeration; (4) BC-2.14.002 — the HTTP mapping VP description referencing "all N categories"; (5) any "N-category axis" prose in cross-cutting docs (ADR-020 §E-TOOLS-008 adjudication, VP-013 §BC Contradictions Flagged, verification-architecture §VP-013 RESOLVED block); **(6) domain-spec/entities-server.md §PregolyaError — the L2 entity definition of the Category type** (EXTENDED burst-315/D-185: burst-308 EXEC sweep correctly updated error-taxonomy + ADR-010 + BC-2.14.001/002 + ADR-020/VP-013/verification-architecture but missed the L2 entity definition in entities-server.md which independently defines the Category variant list; this site has equal authority to BC-2.14.001 from the domain perspective; any future category-axis expansion MUST include entities-server.md §PregolyaError in the mandatory sweep checklist). The class of missed sites is: ADR bodies that REFERENCE the category count in prose or adjudication decisions, verification-architecture RESOLVED blocks that embed a historical count assertion, AND the L2 domain-spec entity definition of the Category type (this lesson's extension). This is the same systematic-miss pattern as L-184 (canonical-form architecture-layer siblings) and L-185 (count-carrier PRD-layer). ROOT CAUSE: specialists correctly updated the primary registry (error-taxonomy) but did not sweep the ADR/verification-architecture prose that mirrors the axis count or the L2 entity definition; the propagation gap surfaced as P1D-200 findings F-P200-01/02/03/04 (original) and F-AUD-C-02 (burst-315 extension). REMEDIATION: orchestrator dispatch for any axis-expansion burst MUST include the full carrier set (error-taxonomy + ADR-010 + BC-2.14.001 + BC-2.14.002 + ADR-020 + VP-013 + verification-architecture + **entities-server.md §PregolyaError**) in the sweep specification. Cross-reference DEFER-004 (proposed mechanical axis/count lint). Tag: `axis-carrier-set`, `category-axis`, `sweep-discipline`, `session-reviewer`. | AXIS-CARRIER propagation discipline — category-axis carrier set includes domain-spec/entities-server.md §PregolyaError (L2 entity definition); ADR bodies, verification-architecture RESOLVED blocks, and domain-spec entity definitions are systematically-missed sibling sites | Codified (P1D-200 F-P200-01/02/03/04, burst-308, D-178, 2026-08-17; EXTENDED burst-315/D-185, 2026-08-17) |
| L-185 | **[PROCESS-GAP / SWEEP-DISCIPLINE]** The COUNT-CARRIER SET for any BC/CAP/DI/VP/ADR/error-code count change is broader than the four *-INDEX files. The full set is: BC-INDEX + L2-INDEX + ARCH-INDEX + VP-INDEX + test-vectors + **prd.md §7 RTM (one row per BC) + prd.md §7 totals + prd.md §5b BC file count + bc-authoring-plan.md frontmatter total_bcs/p1_count/p2_count + bc-authoring-plan.md Summary row + bc-authoring-plan.md Batch tables + STATE.md live tally**. P1D-198 found both prd.md and bc-authoring-plan.md stale from the burst-302b LCEL expansion (129→133 BCs; 75→79 P1) — burst-302b's index-sync step updated the four *-INDEX files but missed the PRD-layer carriers. This is the THIRD instance of the sibling-sweep-under-scoping class (L-172 generalization). ROOT CAUSE: state-manager burst templates list the four *-INDEX files as the canonical count-carrier set; the PRD-layer carriers are not listed. REMEDIATION: orchestrator and state-manager MUST include prd.md §7 RTM + §7 totals + §5b + bc-authoring-plan.md frontmatter/Summary/Batch + STATE.md live tally in EVERY count-change sweep checklist, not just the *-INDEX files. Cross-reference DEFER-004 (proposed validate-count-propagation lint extension to cover RTM row-completeness). Tag: `count-carrier-set`, `prd-layer-sweep`, `session-reviewer`. | Count-carrier set completeness — PRD-layer carriers (prd.md §7 RTM/totals/§5b + bc-authoring-plan frontmatter/Summary/Batch) are mandatory sweep targets alongside *-INDEX files | Codified (F-P198-01/02 / burst-306 / D-176, 2026-08-17) |
| L-187 | **[PROCESS-GAP / SWEEP-DISCIPLINE]** VP formal content is MIRRORED in three places — `verification-properties/VP-XXX.md` (formal invariant + §Source Contract), `architecture/verification-architecture.md` (Provable Properties Catalog + VP detail/formal statement), and `architecture/verification-coverage-matrix.md` (VP-to-Module row). Any correction to a VP's formal statement, argument types, or ADR §-citations MUST sweep ALL THREE mirror sites (not just the VP-XXX.md file). This is the VP-mirror extension of the carrier/sibling-sweep disciplines (L-184/185/186): L-184 identified the architecture-layer sibling miss for canonical-form token changes; L-185 identified PRD-layer carrier misses for count changes; L-186 identified AXIS-CARRIER propagation for taxonomy additions; L-187 (this lesson) identifies VP-mirror-site misses for VP formal content changes. EVIDENCE: F-P204-01 (MED) — VP-014 §Source Contract in VP-014.md had §Decision 1/2 split applied by burst-313 but the mirror copy in verification-architecture.md §VP-014 formal statement still cited the uncorrected form (OBS-P202-B); F-P204-02 (MED) — verification-architecture.md §VP-014 formal statement still used IndexMap as the new() argument type after OBS-P202-B applied the fix to VP-014.md §FormalInvariant. Both were un-swept mirror-siblings of burst-311/312 fixes. REMEDIATION: any fix-burst that edits a VP formal invariant, §Source Contract ADR anchor, or argument type MUST check all three mirror sites: (1) VP-XXX.md (primary); (2) verification-architecture.md §VP-NNN section; (3) verification-coverage-matrix.md VP-to-Module row. Cross-reference DEFER-004 (proposed mechanical lint). Tag: `vp-mirror-sites`, `sweep-discipline`, `session-reviewer`. | VP-mirror-site extension — VP formal content mirrors across VP-XXX.md + verification-architecture.md + verification-coverage-matrix.md; all three must be swept on any VP formal change | Codified (F-P204-01/02 / burst-313 / D-183, 2026-08-17) |
| L-140 | Reconciliation targets, not floors, for bucket sums — requiring "bucket sum must equal the independently measured total (351)" catches both over-counting and under-counting; a floor ("at least N") is satisfiable by a broken discriminator that mis-classifies some bucket; a ceiling ("at most N") would pass in the opposite error direction; the only correct acceptance criterion for a bucket-sum validator is exact equality to an independently measured reference count; pre-wave CLASS3_VALID=208 became 213 post-wave because Wave B shifted 5 openers from violation buckets to the valid bucket — exact equality correctly tracked the redistribution | Reconciliation-target bucket discipline | Codified (verify-error-notation-canon.sh bucket-sum acceptance, burst-282 Wave B, 2026-07-29) |
| L-141 | A fix that legitimately quotes the forbidden form inflates naive grep counts — post-Wave-B corpus greps for the forbidden forms (`...` three-dot and bare struct literal) return hits because changelog entries in the fixed BC files quote the old form to document what changed; the validator's bucket decomposition distinguishes normative text from exempted regions (changelogs, discriminator illustration blocks); a post-wave grep without the discriminator is unreliable for measuring residual violations and must not be substituted for the validator; this is a structural recurrence of L-141 root cause: the fix and the forbidden form share the same token | Fix-quoting-inflation discipline | Codified (D-87 defect 3, post-wave grep unreliability, burst-282 Wave B, 2026-07-29) |
| L-142 | Any path, count, or identifier written into a decision row must be copied verbatim from a command's actual output at authoring time — two consecutive decision rows in two consecutive bursts contained factual errors caught only by post-commit orchestrator verification: D-83 stated "7 existing = 8 total" blocking validators (wrong; correct was 7 total); D-90 stated `artifact-path-registry.yaml` resided in `specs/architecture/` (wrong; it resides at `.factory/` top-level); both values were restated from recollection rather than read from command output; the corrective is mechanical: run the lookup command, paste the output directly, do not paraphrase | Decision-row facts from command output, never recollection | Codified (D-90 path correction, records-only micro-burst, 2026-07-29) |
| L-143 | Any corpus-wide sweep scope MUST be derived from a mechanical enumeration with the command recorded for reproducibility — the burst-283 rename scope was built from recollection and omitted `.factory/planning/` (14 files), `.factory/comparative/` (13 files), `.factory/proposals/` (1 file), and three `.factory/` root files; the corrected manifest measures 598 files / 6,397 occurrences vs the recollection-based partial scope; this is D-87 defect 4 repeating and confirms that sibling-cross-check advice (L-138) without a mechanical enumeration requirement is insufficient; record the enumeration command in the manifest itself | Mechanical-enumeration-over-recollection for sweep scopes | Codified (burst-283 rename sweep manifest correction, D-99, 2026-07-30) |
| L-144 | Closure records are unreliable in both directions — burst-283 found four CRIT findings false-open (routing-spec carried `status: open` after product-owner had applied all changes; sweep-manifest omitted the BC files it touched), and also found D-90/D-83/D-78 were all premise errors ratified into blocking issues before post-hoc verification; false-open and false-closed errors have now both been observed; the only trustworthy source of ground truth is the artifact body itself; closure records and status frontmatter are bookkeeping proxies that must be verified against the artifact, not trusted as authoritative | Records unreliable in both directions — artifact bodies are the only trustworthy source | Codified (burst-283 false-open/false-closed reconciliation, D-98, 2026-07-30) |
| L-145 | A gate that exists but is configured not to block certifies nothing — `validate-artifact-path` runs on every dispatch, logs "graceful degrade", and exits 0 regardless of registry content; its presence in the hook chain created the false impression that path registration was enforced when in fact zero registrations were verified; a gate configured to pass on failure is identical to having no gate; before citing any gate as a control, confirm it can distinguish a valid state from an invalid one | Gate-existence vs gate-effectiveness discipline | Codified (TDIV-008 guard inert, D-94, 2026-07-30) |
| L-146 | Verify the premise before building a blocking-issue theory on it — D-78 (policies.yaml blocked by engine registry), D-83 (validator count from grep-c hitting function definition), D-90 (registry path from recollection), and TDIV-008 (guard reads engine-bundled not project-local) were all plausible premises that turned out to be wrong; each was ratified into a decision row or blocking issue before post-hoc verification; the corrective sequence is: form hypothesis → run the mechanical check → THEN record the conclusion; recording the hypothesis as a conclusion inverts the sequence and produces false blocking issues that cost subsequent debugging | Premise-verification-before-ratification discipline | Codified (D-78/D-83/D-90/TDIV-008 premise errors, burst-283 reconciliation, 2026-07-30) |
| L-147 | An adversary finding's cited BC ID may not be the artifact where the defect actually resides — F-P175-C113 cited BC-2.12.005 but the fabrication was in BC-2.12.002; the cited BC was correct and unchanged; the underlying defect (`RunnableConfig` had no field able to express model/tools/system-prompt, making the Assistant reusable-persona premise incoherent) was real and required ADR-021 + BC-2.12.002 changes; an adversary's target citation is a hypothesis, not a fact; before dismissing a finding because "the cited artifact looks correct," verify whether a sibling artifact contains the actual defect | Adversary-cited-location is a hypothesis, not authoritative | Codified (F-P175-C113 mislabelled target / BC-2.12.002 actual defect, ADR-021, burst-283, 2026-07-30) |
| L-148 | Mechanical sweeps un-grandfather every line they touch — records-lint L9b grandfathers pre-existing text and gates newly-authored text, so a mechanical substitution sweep re-authors every matched line, stripping its grandfathered status; a corpus rename thus generates fresh L9b violations wherever any renamed line contained a version pin that was previously grandfathered; this means no "purely mechanical" rename is possible in a lint-grandfathered corpus without also budgeting for owner-routed semantic-repair passes on the un-grandfathered citations; future exception grants for mechanical sweeps must explicitly budget these repair passes as part of the grant | Records-lint grandfathering interacts with mechanical sweeps | Codified (D-104 boundary-unachievable finding, burst-284, 2026-07-30) |
| L-149 | Concurrent writers on a shared worktree need enforced disjoint scope, not instructed disjoint scope — the D-105 lost-write incident and the burst-283 L2-INDEX near-miss both occurred because agents with overlapping file access were asked but not prevented from touching the same files; instruction-level scope boundaries fail under agentic execution because agents generalize their verification criteria to adjacent files; the only reliable isolation is structural: separate worktrees, or disjoint file lists enforced by the dispatch mechanism, not by agent instructions | Structural isolation over instructed isolation for concurrent writers | Codified (D-105 lost-write incident, burst-284, 2026-07-30) |
| L-150 | A placeholder-shaped citation signals pattern-filling rather than reading the target — `§rows` as a section anchor is semantically empty and resolvable to no document heading; when a citation looks like it was generated by pattern-fill rather than derived from reading the referenced artifact, treat it as a TD-VSDD-059 paper-fix tell regardless of whether the lint gate passes; verifying that the forbidden form vanished is not the same as verifying that the replacement is meaningful and resolves to a real section | Placeholder anchor shape is a paper-fix tell | Codified (D-106 fabricated anchors, burst-284, 2026-07-30) |
| L-151 | Verify every instance of a defect class independently of the reporter's enumerated list — devops self-reported 3 fabricated section anchors; independent grep by owning specialists found 8; the discrepancy was 5 undetected instances; TD-VSDD-059 states that agent self-disclosure of risk severity is not authoritative, and this applies equally to agent self-disclosure of instance counts; for any paper-fix class finding, run the grep independently rather than accepting the reported count as complete | Agent self-reported instance counts are not authoritative | Codified (D-106 8-vs-3 fabricated anchors, TD-VSDD-059, burst-284, 2026-07-30) |
| L-152 | Read the artifact, not the report about the artifact — the orchestrator asserted a non-existent divergence between `comparative/` content and the intended rename outcome based on a summary table rather than reading the files directly; the assertion was wrong; this caused devops to revert 11 files of completed architect work (D-105); in a separate incident the orchestrator asserted an ADR-file rule-scoping divergence from a summary before reading the spec; the corrective is to grep actual file contents when making a factual claim about corpus state rather than relying on any intermediate summary, report, or recollection | Read the artifact body, never the report about it | Codified (D-105 lost-write / orchestrator summary-vs-file error, burst-284, 2026-07-30) |
| L-153 | Mechanism-level findings outrank finding-level counts when routing — P1D-176 produced 160 findings but only 5 convergent mechanisms explain why they persist; fixing the 5 mechanisms prevents regeneration of entire classes while fixing individual findings in severity order does not; when an adversary pass identifies a mechanism (a root cause that explains why the same class of defect re-appears after each fix burst), route the mechanism fix first regardless of its individual finding severity; a mechanism fix is leverage, not a finding | Mechanism-first routing discipline | Codified (P1D-176 five convergent mechanisms, D-110..D-114 (sample), 2026-07-30) |
| L-154 | An internal arithmetic identity is not a ground-truth check — test-vectors.md §Grand-Total was self-consistent at 675 (column sums equaled the declared total at every step) while 12 vectors were missing from the ground truth; the Iron Law census summed correctly while wrong in every term; 5 CI required-checks turned green by validating self-presence rather than behavioral correctness; an internal consistency guard that cannot observe external reality will report PASS for any stable lie; every counter and census must be verified against an independently derived source, not against its own derivative | Arithmetic-identity vs ground-truth discipline | Codified (P1D-176 D001/A009/E023, Mechanism 3, D-112, 2026-07-30) |
| L-155 | A convention with three meanings cannot be gated until it is restricted to one — the `§Name` citation convention covers real headings, bold inline labels, and slugified pseudo-anchors; any gate built on the current convention will produce either false positives (treating legitimate inline labels as phantoms) or false negatives (accepting phantom slugs that look like real headings); the gate architecture prerequisite is a convention ADR restricting `§Name` to form-1 only (real Markdown headings); the anti-volatile-pin policy actively produces form-3 pseudo-anchors, so the policy and the gate are in conflict until the convention is restricted; resolve the convention before building the gate | Convention restriction is gate prerequisite discipline | Codified (P1D-176 A039/E001/E004, Mechanism 1, D-110, 2026-07-30) |
| L-156 | `git worktree repair` with no arguments is a silent no-op — the bare invocation produced no output and exited 0 while leaving both worktree linkage files unchanged; only `git worktree repair <explicit-worktree-path>` performed the repair and updated the stored absolute path; a command that appears to succeed while measuring nothing is the same failure shape as D-109's mechanisms and has now been observed in a git builtin; any future worktree relocation must verify both `.git/worktrees/<name>/gitdir` and `.git` files' contents after repair, never trust the repair command's exit code alone | git-worktree-repair explicit-path discipline | Codified (burst-285 container rename, D-116, 2026-07-31) |
| L-157 | Scope completeness must be verified against the enumeration, not the commit — burst-284's commit was internally consistent and two main-repo files short; `CLAUDE.md` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` were listed in the rename sweep manifest under RENAME scope but were never committed because no burst step owned them; the burst reported "353 files committed" which was accurate and incomplete; the corrective is to diff the manifest enumeration against the committed file list before declaring a sweep burst done | Sweep-manifest-vs-commit completeness discipline | Codified (burst-284 two uncommitted main-repo files, D-117, 2026-07-31) |
| L-158 | Factory-obs is not registered for pregolya — the watched-list holds only `vsdd-factory` and `prism`; no `factory-obs` re-registration was needed for the container rename; record so a future session does not chase it as a missing step | Observability registration scope | Codified (burst-285 container rename state record, D-116, 2026-07-31) |
| L-159 | A checkpoint that defers a value is not a durable snapshot — v4.47's HEADS section told the reader to run `git -C .factory log -1 --format=%h` to discover the factory-artifacts SHA rather than stating it; a zero-context resume cannot rely on a deferred lookup; verify-sha-currency.sh exists precisely to catch this: it detected the missing citation as a WARN before the wrap commit; the underlying anti-pattern is the same family as Mechanism 3 (D-112: arithmetic identity without ground truth) — an artifact that describes how to obtain a missing value rather than containing it looks complete at a glance but fails under independent inspection; the same failure appeared previously in D-90 (path restated from recollection) and D-83 (count restated from recollection); always write the value, never an instruction to find it | Durable-snapshot discipline: carry the value, not the lookup instruction | Codified (v4.47 HEADS deferred SHA defect, D-120, burst-286 session wrap, 2026-07-31) |
| L-160 | A count drop in the first calibrated-instrument pass must not be read as convergence evidence — P1D-177 is the first pass run under POL-46 (adversary finding quality requirements); the expected count reduction partly reflects the instrument being fixed (adversary no longer inflating counts with phantom note-closure findings) and partly reflects genuine corpus improvement from burst-287 mechanism fixes; these two contributions are not separable from a single pass; the 3-CLEAN gate requires sustained CLEAN across three consecutive passes, not a one-pass count reduction; record the before/after FP rate when it lands to distinguish instrument improvement from corpus improvement | Calibrated-instrument count drop ≠ convergence evidence | Codified (burst-287 convergence reframing, D-122/D-123, 2026-08-01) |
| L-161 | Note-closure class is 100% false-positive in this corpus — do not route any note-closure finding to a fix agent without first verifying the stated body against the current artifact at the stated frozen HEAD; the class failure mode is the adversary reading a changelog description of past work as a present-state defect; A010 is the canonical instance: ADR-007's original D7 table ("18 crates") was read as current state while "Authoritative current count: 21" on the following lines was ignored; A005 reversed a gap-finding into a completed-propagation claim | Note-closure verification discipline: verify against current body, not changelog | Codified (7/7 FALSE note-closure adjudication, D-122, burst-287, 2026-08-01) |
| L-162 | An adversary that infers corpus content rather than reading it fabricates coordinates — three findings in the 19-finding adjudication sample cited filenames that do not exist (A005: wrong filename suffix; A008: wrong ADR topic; A010: wrong ADR topic); fabricated filenames combined with fabricated §-section citations indicate the adversary is filling plausible coordinates from its model of the corpus rather than from actual reads; treat any finding that cites a filename or section heading as unverified until the target is confirmed to exist at that exact path; do not commit a fix before running `git show HEAD:<path>` on the cited file | Adversary coordinate fabrication: verify file existence and section existence before routing | Codified (three fabricated filenames A005/A008/A010, D-122, burst-287, 2026-08-01) |
| L-163 | A canon-setting artifact must self-pass before commit — POL-47 was codified from five instances in burst-287: (1) ADR-022 §section-anchor-citation-convention shipped a volatile version pin (the TD-VSDD-091 conflict it resolves); (2) ADR-022 inherited the fabricated ~170 count from the finding it exists to prevent; (3) ADR-023 cited nonexistent `§14-public-enums`/`§public-API-enums` and left a standing instruction to edit nonexistent `§compile-fail-gate`; (4) ADR-025 tripped its own signature validator with prohibited-form examples; (5) orchestrator's canon relay reintroduced the Class 1/Class 3 conflation ADR-010 §error-construction-notation was created to fix; the two-step self-check is: run the relevant gate against the draft, verify PASS before committing | Canon-setting artifact self-pass discipline (POL-47) | Codified (5 burst-287 instances, D-135, burst-287, 2026-08-01) |
| L-164 | Build class-awareness before first use of a gate — the error-notation-canon gate's first implementation was class-blind and reported 48 violations of which 26 were legitimate Class 1 `::new()` calls in rust fences (the form the gate was designed to protect); had it been deployed, a corpus sweep would have replaced 26 mandatory constructions with struct literals, breaking Class 1 canon to satisfy a Class 3 gate; the corrective is to implement the discriminator (in this case: fenced code block detection) before first deployment, not after; a gate deployed without a discriminator creates the defect class it was designed to catch | Class-discriminator-before-deployment discipline | Codified (error-notation-canon rebuild, D-128/D-136, burst-287, 2026-08-01) |
| L-165 | Per-agent PASS on a subset does not equal full-suite PASS — four blocking validators were red during burst-287 while every specialist agent truthfully reported PASS on their own subset; the rule is: the orchestrator must run the full pre-commit-validators.sh suite before any burst step can be declared complete; this is the same failure shape as D-83 (count from partial grep) now recurring in the validator suite itself; the minimum viable enforcement is: the state-manager runs the full suite as the final burst step and does not commit until GATE: PASS | Full-suite gate before burst-close | Codified (4 validators red while all agents PASS, D-136, burst-287, 2026-08-01) |
| L-166 | A permanent advisory-suite warning count trains readers to ignore it — `verify-changelog-claim-applied` has had WARN=662 since burst-282; the advisory is correct in its verified subclass (7/7 note-closure findings it identifies are FALSE), which means 662 WARNs at WARN=662 is not a useful signal; leaving it at this level permanently trains anyone running the suite to dismiss advisory output; the remedy is either narrow it to a mechanically decidable subclass or retire it; a warning count that does not change across dozens of bursts is not a control | Advisory-suite warning counts must be actionable or retired | Codified (verify-changelog-claim-applied WARN=662, D-124, burst-287, 2026-08-01) |
| L-167 | A multi-file fix must enumerate all affected files before declaring done — three instances in burst-287: C001 disambiguation reached 5 of 7 sibling BCs (confirmed by full SS-23 read; the other 4 were clean); A009 count updated in module-decomposition but not propagated to 2 dependents (module-criticality.md + purity-boundary-map.md); YAML defect fixed in purity-boundary-map, missed in module-criticality; the pattern: fix applied and verified on the immediate target, sibling files not enumerated; the corrective is to enumerate the affected file set from the spec before the fix, then diff against it after | Multi-file fix enumeration discipline | Codified (A009 census propagation gap + YAML defect propagation gap, D-130, burst-287, 2026-08-01) |
| L-168 | Two burst-number collisions in two bursts confirms the gap is structural, not a typo — burst-285 collided with existing `wrap(burst-285)` (D-120); burst-287 collided with existing `wrap(burst-286)` (D-121); the immediate cause is that each burst is manually numbered without consulting git log; the minimum viable allocator is a records-lint rule that greps `git log --oneline` for the burst label before accepting the wrap commit; a single sequential counter in a sentinel file is the more robust alternative | Burst-number allocator discipline | Codified (second consecutive collision, D-121, burst-287, 2026-08-01) |
| L-169 | Orchestrator self-attributed defects must be recorded in the decision log, not softened — they are the primary calibration input for the next orchestrator session; the five defects in D-122 are recoverable precisely because they were caught and documented: (1) blanket `::new()` prohibition relay (Class 1/3 conflation reintroduced); (2) D004 mis-routed with no owner; (3) A009 unrouted in Wave 1 despite being in backlog; (4) phantom-anchor reliability predicted wrong (80% false, not reliable); (5) WARN count overstated as "over a third unchecked" (actual: 2 genuine silent skips); softening any of these removes the learning signal for recurrence prevention | Orchestrator self-attribution discipline | Codified (5 orchestrator defects, D-122, burst-287, 2026-08-01) |
| L-170 | A verified-clean claim backed by an enumerated-but-incomplete probe set is a false-clean generator — Slice A probed five ADR-024 attack shapes and declared the confinement argument clean; Slice C found a sixth shape (final component as dangling symlink) not in the enumerated list; each of A's five probes was correctly analyzed and correctly rejected; the gap was in the enumeration closure, not in the individual analyses; this is the same failure class as the tooling defect found in E04 (a roster that certifies without counting): both result in a clean verdict on an incomplete measurement; the corrective is to derive the probe set from the attack surface definition before closing the enumeration, not from what the reviewer recalls as having considered | Incomplete-probe as false-clean generator | Codified (A-vs-C adjudication on ADR-024 dangling symlink, D-131, P1D-177, 2026-08-02) |
| L-171 | A policy requirement must be verified against the constrained agent's tool profile before minting — POL-46 requirement 1 mandates inline executed-command output; the adversary agent has Bash denied in its tool profile; every adversary in this project violated POL-46 req-1 on first dispatch because the requirement was authored without checking executability; a policy that cannot be complied with is worse than no policy: it trains agents to note non-compliance rather than to comply, and it obscures genuine quality differences between evidence types; before minting any gate or policy, run the self-validation step: "can the constrained agent produce this artifact?" | Policy-executability-before-minting discipline | Codified (POL-46 req-1 unsatisfiability, D-133, P1D-177, 2026-08-02) |
| L-172 | Partial-fix propagation is a structural defect, not a policy gap — ten instances from burst-287 demonstrate that POL-24 (sibling sweep) is insufficient because it requires sweeping after the fix, not enumerating before; a fix that patches two of five postconditions (C-H01), corrects one of four bc-authoring-plan rows (D-01), or propagates to one of two sibling consumers (C-H02) is structurally similar to a false-closure (Mechanism 2): the fix is applied and the changelog says "done"; the only structural remedy is to enumerate the complete target file set from the spec before any change is applied, then diff the enumeration against the committed change set after; POL-24 should be amended to require pre-fix enumeration, not post-fix sweep | Pre-fix enumeration is the structural remedy for partial-fix propagation | Codified (dominant mechanism of P1D-177, ~10 burst-287 instances, D-134, 2026-08-02) |
| L-173 | Orchestrator dispatch defects compound into adversary instrument defects — the three self-attributed dispatch errors in P1D-177 (Bash-pin instruction, supplement count 10 vs 7, D002 misframing) each affected all five slices; a dispatch error that reaches 5 agents multiplies its impact by 5; a miscount of 3 files (7 vs 10) in the boundary description causes every slice to certify the supplement perimeter exhausted at the wrong count; the misframing of a finding's category (wave/lifecycle vs phantom-crate) biases every slice's search pattern; the corrective for dispatch-level errors is the same as for corpus-level errors: verify the claim from the artifact before putting it in a dispatch prompt, never from a prior summary or report | Dispatch-error multiplication: verify dispatch claims from artifacts | Codified (3 orchestrator dispatch defects, D-135, P1D-177, 2026-08-02) |
| L-174 | The shared-worktree concurrent-writer hazard recurs whenever multiple specialists are dispatched within a single fix-burst — this burst saw (1) devops-engineer attempt a repo-wide `git checkout --` while the worktree was in use, and (2) architect-B spawn an unauthorized state-manager and attempt a premature mid-burst commit; both were blocked by the dispatcher hook chain; neither caused data loss because targeted Edit tools were used rather than broad restores; the structural fix is: fix-burst specialist dispatch prompts must explicitly forbid spawning state-manager and forbid running any git commit/checkout/reset/restore command (only the orchestrator's terminal state-manager dispatch may commit) | Shared-worktree concurrent-writer discipline in fix-burst prompts | Codified (burst-288 D-136, 2026-08-15) |
| L-175 | Validator-derived instruction beats convention-guessing — when changelog direction convention was unclear (ascending vs descending), the correct action was to run verify-form-a-changelog-direction.sh and verify-changelog-direction.sh on a known-good file rather than infer from memory or documentation; the validators produced unambiguous ground truth (Form A: ascending, Form B: descending) that resolved the question in one step; any time a project has a gate for a convention, the gate's verdict is more authoritative than documentation, prior session memory, or in-session reasoning | Gate-as-ground-truth discipline: run the gate, don't guess the convention | Codified (changelog-direction adjudication, D-137, burst-288, 2026-08-15) |
| L-176 | A count stated in N docs with no single-source-of-truth gate is a recurring count-drift source — the StreamEvent variant count (15→16 in burst-288) is the SECOND count-propagation miss in this project (D23 12→15 was the first); both missed ≥6 sibling documents that independently state the same count; per-file changelog-claim gates verify only the file they're run against, not whether sibling files have been updated to match; the structural fix is a cross-doc grep gate that compares count-bearing strings in sibling docs against the authoritative count in BC-2.06.001 §Postconditions PC2 | Cross-doc count drift requires a cross-file grep gate, not per-file changelog gates | Codified (F-178-01 second StreamEvent propagation miss, PG-178-01, burst-289, 2026-08-16) |
| L-177 | Phantom BC-body §anchors recur when the governing ADR (POL-47) is not self-applied at edit time — two burst-288 edits (ADR-023, BC-2.10.003) introduced phantom BC-body §anchors on the same burst that built phantom-anchor governance; the BC-body anchor space is currently machine-unenforced (only ADR-target anchors have a gate via verify-adr-anchor-citations.sh); until a BC-target heading-existence check is added as an advisory hook, authors must manually verify that any `BC-NNN §heading-name` anchor resolves to an actual Markdown heading before committing | BC-target §anchor existence verification discipline (pending advisory hook) | Codified (F-178-03/F-178-04 phantom BC-body anchors, PG-178-02, burst-289, 2026-08-16) |
| L-178 | Fresh-context deep-read of a previously-SAMPLED axis is the highest-value convergence mechanism — P1D-180 is the first pass to deep-read the full ADR-target §-citation corpus rather than sampling it; P1D-179 was CLEAN on its sampled coverage of the same axis; the sampled CLEAN was genuine (no false samples) but the axis had a machine-visible backlog of ~10–12 WARNs that sampling missed; rotating deep-read emphasis across streak passes rather than re-checking items that were already deep-read is more likely to surface the remaining live debt; "previously converged ≠ correct" when the convergence was on a sample | Rotate deep-read emphasis across streak passes; previously-converged ≠ correct | Codified (P1D-180 ADR-citation axis deep-read, D-144, burst-290, 2026-08-16) |
| L-179 | An implemented-but-advisory gate with a nonzero WARN backlog is a false-green vector — verify-adr-anchor-citations.sh was advisory (WARN mode) and POL-19 carried ~10–12 known phantoms as deferred items under ADR-022 Decision 4 without a human-authorized story anchor (violating POL-29/POL-31 deferral requirements); because the gate was advisory, two of ADR-022's own counter-examples (F-180-01 and F-180-02) persisted live through at least two passes including the P1D-179 streak pass; the pattern: a gate that cannot fail is a gate that cannot help; when a validator's output is WARN-only, its WARNs are live debt, not deferred debt — promote-or-fix is the correct posture, not carry-as-advisory-indefinitely | Advisory-gate WARN backlog is live debt: promote-or-fix, not carry-forever | Codified (F-180-PG advisory gate false-green, ADR-022 Decision 4 DISCHARGED, D-146, burst-290, 2026-08-16) |
| L-188 | **[PROCESS-GAP / SWEEP-DISCIPLINE]** Count consistency within a single document does NOT guarantee count correctness across the corpus — P1D-209 accepted module-criticality.md's Classification Summary (84 total, HIGH 29) as internally consistent and CLEAN; P1D-210 performed a cross-document diff between module-criticality.md and verification-coverage-matrix.md and exposed that `core::serializable` was double-counted across CRITICAL and HIGH tier rows (it has a CRITICAL aspect under VP-010/Reviver and a HIGH aspect under VP-007/LcSerializable); the correct canonical count is: one module counted once in its HIGHEST tier = CRITICAL (Resolution B); corrected: total 84→83, HIGH 29→28, tiered 78→77. STRUCTURAL LESSON: a single-document consistency check (all row sums add up, all tier enumerations exhaustive) can pass while a cross-document enumeration diff reveals a double-count invisible to the single-document lens; the "previously converged = correct" assumption (L-178) applies here at the cross-document level: P1D-209's CLEAN verdict was accurate for the single-document axis it covered, but did not certify correctness of the census numbers themselves. REMEDIATION: adversary cascade checkpoints SHOULD include at least one cross-document enumeration diff per streak (e.g., module-criticality tier totals vs coverage-matrix §Coverage-by-Criticality-Tier counts vs module-decomposition §Iron-Law totals) — the three documents form a canonical triangle that should agree; any pass that accepts one apex without cross-checking the other two is an opportunity for a double-count to persist across streaks. Reinforces DEFER-004 (proposed mechanical count-carrier lint). The purity-boundary-map "84 total rows" was verified INDEPENDENT and CORRECT (different universe from module-criticality 83; the purity-partition counts module-decomposition rows including structural entries; this is a DIFFERENT count, not a contradiction). Tag: `count-correctness`, `cross-document-diff`, `single-doc-consistent-not-corpus-correct`, `session-reviewer`. | Cross-document enumeration diff discipline — single-document consistency ≠ count correctness; purity-boundary-map 84 is an independent correct count over a different universe | Codified (F-P210-01 / fix-burst-321 / D-192, 2026-08-18) |
| L-189 | **[ORCHESTRATION-GAP / ESCALATION-ROUTING]** Every story-writer-reported escalation must enter the PO adjudication set — never be judged "already handled" by the orchestrator. In M3a, escalation #11 (S-2.03 similarity score range) was surfaced by a story-writer fork but was not routed to M3c PO adjudication; in batch-6, the S-2.04/S-2.05 escalations were recorded as "corrected" inline by the orchestrator rather than receiving discrete PO review. Both slipped through to P2A-043: F-01 (CRIT) was S-2.03 escalation #11 unrouted — the [−1,1] range had never been corrected to [0.0,1.0]; F-03 were the S-2.04/S-2.05 PO-confirm notes that had never been adjudicated. STRUCTURAL LESSON: the orchestrator is not a substitute for the PO adjudication sub-process. An escalation that the orchestrator believes is "already handled" must still receive an explicit PO verdict with a D-NNN row before the cascade advances. | L-197 | **[PROCESS-GAP / REVIEW-DISCIPLINE]** Rounds 1 through 8 of the Phase-2 re-convergence cascade performed control-flow reachability and seam-type checks on VP-016 (GraphAgentTool state-isolation harness) without ever verifying that the named Rust types, methods, traits, and enum variants used in the contract ACTUALLY EXIST in the codebase. The structural fix for this class: `hooks/verify-no-phantom-types.sh` (corpus-grep advisory gate) mechanically enforces that zero occurrences of the known phantom-type signatures exist across the entire `.factory/` tree; this gate runs on every factory commit going forward. STRUCTURAL LESSON: TD-VSDD-060 sibling-sweep discipline requires corpus-grep over the FULL tree for every type/symbol change — an enumerated file list is a paper-sweep (TD-VSDD-059) because it cannot catch files the enumerator did not anticipate. When a phantom-type finding recurs in a subsequent pass despite a claimed sibling-sweep, the diagnosis is always "the sweep was bounded by human recall, not by mechanical grep." | TD-VSDD-060 sibling-sweep must be corpus-grep not enumerated file list; enumerated-list sweeps leave stragglers; verify-no-phantom-types.sh mechanically enforces completeness | Codified (P2A-075/076/077/round-12/D-285, hooks/verify-no-phantom-types.sh, corpus-grep pattern, 2026-08-27) |

| L-200 | **[PROCESS-GAP / SEMANTIC-RECONCILIATION DISCIPLINE]** [codified] Rounds 10 and 12 of the Phase-2 re-convergence cascade used token-grep sibling-sweeps to close the GAP-01 phantom-type class: `rg 'ToolOutput::Structured\|CompiledGraph<\|as_value()\|&S\|S: GraphState' .factory/`. These greps are structurally correct for closing CODE-TOKEN phantom occurrences — they catch exact Rust identifier strings wherever they appear. However, round-13 (P2A-078/079/080) discovered that multiple documents still carried GAP-01 semantics in forms that code-token greps CANNOT catch: (1) PROSE forms — "GraphState S", "ToolOutput returned by invoke_dyn", "compiled StateGraph" (natural-language descriptions that convey the phantom semantics without using the exact token); (2) ENUM-ENUMERATION forms — `ActionRisk: None \| Low \| Medium \| High` in a symbol-grounding table (None is a valid Option::None context but was listed as an ActionRisk variant, implying an undeclared Deny case); (3) STRUCT-FIELD forms — verification-architecture.md VP-016 harness struct field names that reflected the old TestGraphState struct rather than the new serde_json::Value form; (4) FILENAME/REFERENCE forms — dangling `VP-016.md` bare-form filename citation in a context where the canonical slug form `vp-016-graph-agent-tool-state-isolation.md` was required; (5) TRACEABILITY-LABEL forms — `EC-TV-1` used in a §BC-Traceability section instead of canonical `TV-001 / EC-007`. STRUCTURAL LESSON: a large type-grounding migration requires BOTH a mechanical corpus-grep (handled by verify-no-phantom-types.sh) AND a semantic per-document reconciliation pass for each affected document. The semantic pass reconciles every prose description, enum enumeration, struct-field name, filename citation, and traceability-label reference against the new canonical design — not just the code-token occurrences. The closing gate must check the TYPE NAME in Task lists, prose descriptions, and traceability tables — not only the eliminated Rust variant identifier. | L-201 | **[PROCESS-GAP / SWEEP-DISCIPLINE]** [codified] Rounds 13-15 of the Phase-2 re-convergence cascade each surfaced ONE shrinking sibling-mirror residue of the same class: explanatory prose in §Rationale and §Why-proptest sections that described phantom design semantics ("arbitrary GraphState instances", "graph's GraphState is an accumulator type", "MUST NOT embed credential material in ToolOutput") while the normative sections had already been corrected. Each round used a targeted grep for the specific failing token, which fixed NORMATIVE sections but left EXPLANATORY prose intact — because explanatory prose uses natural language that token-greps do not match. The terminating fix in round-16 was a FULL-DOCUMENT enumerate-classify-fix pass over ADR-029: every occurrence of `ToolOutput`, `GraphState`, and `TestGraphState` was classified as (a) legitimate in-graph usage (ToolOutput::Error in node-receives paths — KEEP), (b) stale invoke_dyn-return reference (REPLACE with serde_json::Value), or (c) explanatory-prose mirror that needed updating to match the normative section (REPLACE with current design language). Only the full-document classification pass terminated the class. STRUCTURAL LESSON: after a type-grounding migration, do ONE full-document semantic pass over each authored ADR (covering ALL sections including §Rationale and §Why explanatory prose), not section-targeted sweeps. Explanatory-prose mirrors lag behind normative sections because token-greps match identifiers, not semantics. The classification step — legitimate vs stale vs mirror — is the key discipline; without it, a targeted grep is structurally unable to find all residue. | After type-grounding migration: full-document enumerate-classify-fix pass over each ADR; section-targeted sweeps leave explanatory-prose mirrors behind; classify ToolOutput::Error (legitimate) vs invoke_dyn-return ToolOutput (stale) before fixing | Codified (P2A-081/082/083 round-16/D-287, ADR-029 full-document pass, explanatory-prose mirror class terminated, 2026-08-27) |
| L-202 | **[ARCHITECTURE / TEST-UTILITY VISIBILITY]** [codified] Round-21 (F-P2A093-01) found that VP-016's proof harness required a `stub_terminal` helper from pregolya-graph, which had been gated with bare `#[cfg(test)]` after its routing to S-1.14 AC-014/Task-18 in round-10. In Rust, `#[cfg(test)]` items are compiled only when the OWNING crate is under test — they are invisible to dependent crates' test builds (E0599: no method named `stub_terminal` found for type `CompiledStateGraph`). The claim in §Proof-Harness and §Proof-Obligations that stub_terminal was "accessible as a dev-dependency" was therefore false. The correct pattern for cross-crate test helpers is a dedicated `test-util` Cargo feature: `[features] test-util = []` in pregolya-graph, `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "test-util"))] pub fn stub_terminal(...)` on the helper, and `pregolya-graph = { ..., features = ["test-util"] }` in pregolya-mcp's `[dev-dependencies]`. This pattern exposes the helper ONLY to crates that explicitly opt in; it does not add the helper to release builds because `features = ["test-util"]` is dev-only. STRUCTURAL LESSON: any test helper that must be used by a dependent crate's tests MUST use a test-util feature gate — bare `#[cfg(test)]` is not sufficient. When routing a helper to a story spec (AC/Task), the AC must specify the feature-gate mechanism explicitly, not just "add a #[cfg(test)] helper." The §Proof-Obligations section of each VP that relies on a cross-crate test seam must cite the feature gate by name. | Cross-crate cfg(test) items are compiler-invisible; use test-util feature gate for helpers needed by dependent crates' tests | Codified (F-P2A093-01/round-21/D-291, pregolya-graph test-util feature, S-1.14 AC-014 prerequisite corrected, S-2.11 Task-27 dev-dep wiring, vp-016 §Proof-Harness corrected, 2026-08-28) |
| L-203 | **[PROCESS-GAP / TYPE-CORRECTION MIRROR-IMAGE RISK]** [codified] Round-19 (F-P2A088-01) corrected a sanitizer-scope error in BC-2.09.008 {INV-001}: the type-grounding work in rounds 10-12 had changed wording in ways that implied `thread_id` was an arbitrary string, when the canonical type is Uuid per entities-server §Thread/§Run. The fix re-grounded the sanitizer scope: authoring-site convention SOLE-guarantee applies to u64 `CheckpointId` ONLY; the framework UUID regex covers BOTH `run_id` AND `server thread_id`. Round-21 (F-P2A094-01) found that the round-19 fix introduced a MIRROR-IMAGE error on an ADJACENT identifier in the SAME contract: after establishing that `thread_id` is Uuid (and therefore framework-covered), the {INV-001} prose and ADR-029 §Decision-3/SEC-005 text was re-read and found to still claim that the authoring-site convention SOLE-guarantee covered `thread_id` — the opposite of the intent. This is a mirror-image error: the correction of one identifier's premise introduces an implicit contradiction in the adjacent identifier's statement. STRUCTURAL LESSON: when a type-premise correction is applied to one identifier in a contract, IMMEDIATELY re-verify ALL sibling identifiers in the same clause against the SAME canonical type authority. A single identifier correction that changes from "arbitrary string" to "Uuid" creates an asymmetry — the claim that was true before the correction (authoring-site-convention covers X) now needs re-examination for every X in the same clause. The fix protocol must include a "sibling-identifier re-verification" step before closing the finding. This is an instance of TD-VSDD-060 (sibling-site sweep) applied to spec-level type claims, not just code callsites. | Type-premise correction on one identifier may introduce mirror-image error on adjacent identifiers in same clause; re-verify ALL sibling identifiers against canonical type authority | Codified (F-P2A094-01/round-21/D-291, mirror-of-F-P2A088-01/round-19, ADR-029 §Decision-3/SEC-005 + BC-2.09.008 {INV-001} corrected, TD-VSDD-060 spec-level variant, 2026-08-28) |
| L-204 | **[PROCESS-GAP / PARTIAL-BACKFILL DISCIPLINE]** [codified] Round-20 (F-P2A092-03) backfilled the VP column in module-criticality.md for two VP-hosting modules (mcp::graph_tool→VP-016 and mcp::sanitize→VP-015) after the round-19 GATE-READY audit flagged them. The correct procedure for a VP-column backfill is: (1) enumerate the VP-INDEX authority list of all VPs with their module assignments; (2) for each VP, verify the corresponding module's row in module-criticality.md has the VP cited; (3) fix ALL missing cells in a single burst. F-P2A095-02 (POL-29) closed the round-20 self-authorization of a deferral for work that was clearly answerable in scope. The new verify-module-criticality-vp-column.sh gate (advisory) prevents future partial-backfill regressions by cross-checking VP-INDEX (VP,module) pairs against the module-criticality VP column on every factory commit. | Partial table/column backfills beget follow-on findings; complete backfill requires cross-checking every source-of-truth row not just flagged cells; orchestrator must not self-authorize deferrals for answerable-in-scope work | Codified (F-P2A095-01/F-P2A095-02/round-21/D-291, module-criticality VP-column exhaustive backfill, verify-module-criticality-vp-column.sh gate, POL-29 deferral-authorization rule, 2026-08-28) |

STRUCTURAL LESSON: holdout scenario files MUST maintain two distinct namespaces: (1) internal IDs (scenario ID, version, factory metadata — for factory tracking only, not evaluator-visible); (2) evaluator-facing content (scenario description, acceptance criteria, domain context — must be clean of any internal artifact identifiers). When authoring or revising holdout scenarios, the §Acceptance-Criteria section must be audited for any string matching `HS-.*`, `F-P2A.*`, or similar factory-internal ID patterns. STRUCTURAL LESSON: the obligation "story AC asserts mechanism X → BC governing X must carry contract for X" is bidirectional. When a story-writer adds an AC for a mechanism, the orchestrator MUST also dispatch the product-owner to verify the governing BC carries the asserted contract. If the BC does not, a single-burst fix (story + BC in same atomic commit) is mandatory. The backfill is not "complete" until the verifier exits 0. | L-220 | **[PROCESS-GAP / EXHAUSTIVE-SWEEP DISCIPLINE — 4TH OCCURRENCE (ROUNDS 27/28/29)]** [codified] Round-29 (F-P2A125-01 [HIGH], F-P2A127-01 [MED]) is the FOURTH consecutive session occurrence of the same sub-class of INCOMPLETE-SWEEP SIBLING-MIRROR: a canonical-form change (regex, type spec, identifier, count) was fixed at the CITED LOCATION but NOT swept corpus-wide to all normative siblings carrying the same canonical form in the same burst. Round-27: CRITICAL→ERROR fix missed 5th sibling (interface-definitions doc-comment). Round-28: two-pattern sanitizer union applied to BC-2.09.008 but NOT to ADR-029 §Decision 3/5 (architectural source-of-truth carrying the same SEC-005 pattern); ToolRegistry type fix applied to module-decomp but NOT to S-2.11 §File-Structure (6th occurrence). Round-29: same two omissions confirmed by P2A-125 + P2A-127. The defining invariant of this sub-class: "when a canonical form (regex/type spec/identifier) changes, EVERY normative document that CITES that form must carry the update." The finding-citation heuristic (fix only the document named in the finding) systematically misses documents that CARRY the form but are not named in the finding because they were not sampled by the adversary. STRUCTURAL LESSON (4th occurrence — machine gate required per L-213): the fix protocol for ANY canonical-form change must include a two-step procedure BEFORE the burst commit: (1) run `grep -r '<old_canonical_form>' .factory/` to find all occurrences; (2) update EVERY occurrence that is normative (not commentary or historical). The invariant "zero occurrences of the old form in normative content" is the exit criterion, not "the finding's cited location is fixed." | L-221 | **[OPERATIONAL / BURST-PRE-SPLIT DISCIPLINE FOR LARGE AGENT TASKS]** [codified] This session saw 2 mid-burst agent stalls: round-26 (product-owner given BC-2.09.007 + BC-2.09.008 across many sections) and round-29 (story-writer given 4 findings across S-1.19 + S-2.11). Both stalls occurred when ONE agent was given a task spanning >~4 findings or >~3 files. Stalled agents produce no output and require a full re-dispatch with smaller scope, adding a round-trip to the cascade. OPERATIONAL LESSON: when composing a fix-burst agent task, the orchestrator MUST assess scope before dispatch: (a) if the task involves >~4 distinct findings, split by finding-set (e.g., 2 agents of ~2 findings each); (b) if the task involves >~3 files with independent changes, split by file-group; (c) if both conditions hold, split both ways. The pre-split adds 1-2 agent dispatches but eliminates stall + re-dispatch overhead which is always larger. The heuristic is conservative: splitting a 3-finding task into 2+1 is cheaper than one stall. Owner: orchestrator dispatch discipline. Candidate: add a scope-check step to the orchestrator pre-burst checklist. | Mid-burst agent stalls occur when one agent has >~4 findings or >~3 files; pre-split large tasks into smaller agent sub-tasks before dispatch to avoid stall overhead; rounds 26 + 29 both exhibited this pattern | Codified (round-26 PO stall + round-29 story-writer stall/D-299, burst-pre-split discipline, orchestrator checklist candidate, 2026-08-28) |
| L-220 | **[PROCESS-GAP / BC-CLAUSE-STORY-AC-RE-ANCHOR DISCIPLINE — 3RD OCCURRENCE]** [codified] Round-28 (F-P2A123-03 [MED]) is the THIRD occurrence of the BC-clause/story-AC re-anchor pattern: S-1.19 AC-024 was anchored to BC-2.09.008 PC-001 (which "mentions" the FutureExt::catch_unwind obligation as a call-site reference) but the correct governing BC is BC-2.11.002 {INV-005} (which GOVERNS the FutureExt mechanism as an invariant for the SS-11 provenance subsystem). The re-anchor obligation: when an AC traces a mechanism, it must trace to the BC that GOVERNS the mechanism (defines it as an invariant or precondition), not the BC that merely mentions or references the mechanism as a side-note. This is the same structural error as round-27 (L-216: orphaned-AC story-to-BC anchor) and round-26 (L-214: BC-2.09.008 catch_unwind 5th-sibling), confirming a recurring routing pattern. STRUCTURAL LESSON (codifying beyond L-216): when a PRODUCT-OWNER or STORY-WRITER adds an AC referencing a mechanism that spans multiple BCs, the triage protocol is: (1) identify ALL BCs that reference the mechanism (grep by mechanism name/type); (2) among those BCs, classify: which GOVERNS the mechanism (invariant/precondition/constraint) vs which OBSERVES/REFERENCES it (call-site, implication, side-note); (3) the story AC must trace to the GOVERNING BC, and the GOVERNING BC must carry the corresponding contract clause in the same burst. A 3rd-occurrence classification triggers escalation to a machine gate (PROCESS-GAP CANDIDATE: `verify-ac-governs-bc-alignment.sh` — cross-checks story AC mechanism references against BC invariant ownership). This is a first self-improvement wave candidate; NOT a silent defer. | 3rd occurrence of BC-clause/story-AC re-anchor: S-1.19 AC-024 traced to BC-2.09.008 (mentions FutureExt) instead of BC-2.11.002 {INV-005} (governs FutureExt); trace-to-governing-BC rule; 3rd-occurrence escalates to machine-gate candidate | Codified (F-P2A123-03[MED]/round-28/D-298, S-1.19 AC-024 BC-2.09.008→BC-2.11.002 {INV-005} re-anchor, governs-vs-mentions distinction, 3rd-occurrence gate escalation, 2026-08-28) |
| L-222 | **[PROCESS-GAP / COMPOSE-PASSES-NOT-JUST-ISOLATE]** [codified] Round-30 (F-P2A129-01 [MED, CWE-209]) surfaced a defect (TV-015 pipeline-composition) that survived rounds 25-29 because every prior adversary pass examined the sanitize_internal_ids mechanism in ISOLATION — testing it against an input that had NOT been pre-processed by redact_credentials. In the actual execution pipeline, redact_credentials runs FIRST and its regex `[A-Za-z0-9]{64,}` matches and redacts 64-character hex digests that appear in content text BEFORE sanitize_internal_ids has a chance to act. TV-015 was testing sanitize_internal_ids with a 64-char hex input, but in the composed pipeline the redact_credentials pass would have already replaced it with `<redacted>`, changing the expected output. The defect was invisible to isolation testing but immediately visible to composition testing. STRUCTURAL LESSON: sanitizer and filter mechanisms that run in a PIPELINE must be tested as a COMPOSITION, not just in isolation. For any pipeline where stage N can transform the input before stage N+1 consumes it, adversarial test vectors must include cross-stage interaction vectors (TV-017 was added as an append-only isolation vector for sanitize_internal_ids alone; TV-015 corrected to reflect the composed execution order). The {INV-001} interaction note added to BC-2.09.008 formalizes the "redact_credentials runs first" invariant so future adversary passes can probe compositions rather than stages in isolation. PROCESS-GAP CANDIDATE: when BC {INV} blocks assert execution-order invariants, adversarial passes MUST include at least one cross-stage composition TV per pipeline interaction noted in {INV}. Owner: product-owner/adversary at BC authoring time. | TV-015 pipeline-composition defect survived 5 rounds because prior passes tested sanitize_internal_ids in isolation — not composed with redact_credentials which runs first; sanitizer pipelines must be tested as compositions, not just isolated stages; {INV-001} interaction note + TV-017 isolation vector added | Codified (F-P2A129-01[MED,CWE-209]/round-30/D-302, BC-2.09.008 {INV-001} pipeline-interaction note, TV-015 corrected, TV-017 append-only isolation vector, 2026-08-28) |

| L-224 | **[PROCESS-GAP / SIBLING-TEXT-EQUALITY IS NOT CORROBORATION]** [codified] Round-31 (F-P2A135-01 [HIGH]) found that the mcp::registry "mcp::client populates at session startup" phantom claim survived round-30 because P2A-131 and all prior passes used a text-equality perimeter: they checked that the three architecture-triad siblings (module-decomposition, purity-boundary-map, vcm) and S-2.11 all carried the same consumer-set language — and they did. But the language was wrong in all four, synchronized to the phantom via a round-30 adjudication that correctly stated "mcp::client writes/populates" but also stated "at session startup" (a timeline claim with NO story anchor). P2A-135 discovered the phantom by checking the STORY (S-2.10, S-2.11) ACs/Tasks and the governing BC contracts (BC-2.09.006 PRE-001, BC-2.09.008 PC-002): S-2.10 returns a Vec to the caller; no BC references a "session startup" registration event; the actual registration-API caller is the application layer per BC-2.09.006 {PRE-001} + BC-2.09.008 {PC-002}. The text-equality perimeter was structurally blind to this class because it could only detect disagreement between siblings — it could not detect that all siblings were synchronized to a false claim. STRUCTURAL LESSON: when verifying a module-responsibility or registrar-role claim (especially "MODULE X populates/writes/registers"), sibling-text-equality is a NECESSARY but NOT SUFFICIENT check. The SUFFICIENT check requires: (1) identify the module named as producer/registrar; (2) find that module's anchor story; (3) confirm the anchor story's ACs or Tasks contain an AC/Task implementing the stated responsibility; (4) confirm the governing BC contract for that responsibility exists and names the correct caller. A claim that passes text-equality but fails story-anchor-corroboration is a phantom. PROCESS-GAP CANDIDATE: POL-24-COMPANION-STORY-CORROBORATION — when a module-responsibility/registrar phrase names a producer module, a validator or adversary companion step must confirm that module's anchor story contains an AC/Task implementing the responsibility. | L-225 | **[OPERATIONAL / PROPTEST ASYNC CONTROL FLOW — prop_assert! MUST NOT RUN INSIDE block_on(async{})]** [codified; extended round-32] Round-31 (F-P2A132-01 [HIGH]) found that VP-016 §Feasibility contained a non-compiling + false-green proptest harness: `prop_assert!` and `prop_assert_eq!` were placed inside a `rt.block_on(async { ... })` closure whose return value was not bound or checked. The root cause has two parts: (1) `prop_assert!` (and `prop_assert_eq!`) expand to `return Err(TestCaseError::Fail(...))` — this `return` exits the CLOSURE, not the test function, meaning it propagates to the `block_on` return value as an `Err(TestCaseError)`, which was silently discarded. The test could never report a failure even when an assertion failed. (2) The `block_on(async { ... })` return type was discarded, creating a false-green: the async block received the error but the outer test function ignored it, so every proptest case "passed" regardless of assertion outcome. STRUCTURAL FIX applied in VP-016 §Feasibility: setup and all assertions hoisted to the `#[test] fn` body; only the `async { tool.invoke_dyn(input).await }` call itself runs inside `rt.block_on(...)`; the result is bound at body level (`let result = rt.block_on(...)`); assertions run against `result` at body scope. GENERAL RULE: in a proptest `#[test] fn`, NEVER place `prop_assert!`/`prop_assert_eq!`/`prop_assume!` inside any closure (async or sync) whose `Result` is not directly returned to the proptest runner. The proptest macros use `return Err(TestCaseError)` for flow control; any closure that intercepts this `return` before it reaches the test function creates a false-green harness. The only correct location for proptest assertions is the test function body itself (or a non-closure helper that `?`-propagates). EXTENSION (round-32/D-304): the SIBLING-SWEEP corollary — when a harness-idiom defect (e.g., prop_assert!-inside-block_on) is fixed in one VP, ALL VPs of the same test-tool class (all proptest VPs, all Kani VPs) MUST be swept in the SAME burst for the identical pattern. Round-31 fixed VP-016's proptest harness but did NOT sweep VP-014, which had the byte-identical defect; VP-014 was discovered only in round-32 as F-P2A136-02[HIGH] — a round-31 sibling-propagation MISS. Exhaustive post-fix sweep for round-32 confirmed VP-006-B/007/008 were already-clean (pure-sync harnesses, no block_on) and VP-014 was the lone straggler. The fix discipline for harness-idiom defects: (1) identify the test-tool class (proptest, Kani, cargo-fuzz); (2) grep for the defective pattern across ALL VPs in that class (e.g., `grep -r 'block_on' .factory/specs/verification-properties/`); (3) apply the fix to EVERY VP that exhibits the pattern in the same burst. PROCESS-GAP CANDIDATE: HARNESS-IDIOM-CLASS-SWEEP — when an adversary pass finds a harness-idiom defect in VP-NNN, a companion step must explicitly sweep all VPs of the same test-tool class. Owner: devops-engineer/architect. Target: first-self-improvement-wave. This is NOT a silent defer — explicit target and owner recorded. | prop_assert!/prop_assert_eq! expand to return Err(TestCaseError) — placing them inside block_on(async{}) whose Result is discarded creates a false-green harness; always hoist proptest assertions to #[test] fn body level; EXTENSION: when fixing a harness-idiom defect in one VP, sweep ALL VPs of the same test-tool class in the SAME burst (R31 fixed VP-016 but missed VP-014; found R32 as F-P2A136-02[HIGH]) | Codified (F-P2A132-01[HIGH]/round-31/D-303, VP-016 §Feasibility proptest false-green, prop_assert! control-flow semantics, 2026-08-28); Extended (F-P2A136-02[HIGH]/round-32/D-304, VP-014 proptest false-green same defect, HARNESS-IDIOM-CLASS-SWEEP discipline, proptest-VP exhaustive sweep VP-006-B/007/008 clean+VP-014 lone straggler, 2026-08-29) |

PROCESS-GAP CANDIDATE: OBJECT-SAFETY-AUDIT-GATE — a validator confirming every trait with `async fn` used behind `dyn` carries `#[async_trait]`, and every impl of such a trait carries it. | L-228 | **[OPERATIONAL / ASYNC-TRAIT-REALIZABILITY-STACK — OBJECT-SAFE ASYNC DISPATCH IS A MULTI-LAYER REALIZABILITY PROBLEM]** [codified] Round-34 (F-P2A144-01 [HIGH]) found that blanket `impl DynTool for T where T: Tool` and `impl DynRunnable for T where T: Runnable` were non-realizable on stable Rust because the generic type parameter `T`'s RPITIT future return from the native-async `Tool`/`Runnable` supertraits was not provably `Send`. The compiler (E0277) rejects these blanket impls because `#[async_trait]` on the façade trait boxes the future as `Box<dyn Future + Send>`, but the generic `T: Runnable`'s RPITIT future (from `async fn invoke(...)`) is not constrained to be `Send` — `T` could be implemented by a type whose future is `!Send`. The RPITIT (Return-Position Impl Trait In Traits, stabilized Rust 2024) form `async fn foo() -> T` desugars to `fn foo() -> impl Future<Output = T>`, which is opaque and not `Send`-bounded by default. The unstable RTN (Return Type Notation) feature is the ONLY alternative on nightly. **STRUCTURAL LESSON:** object-safe async dispatch is a TWO-LAYER realizability stack: (layer 1) the façade trait (e.g., `DynRunnable`, `DynTool`) must carry `#[async_trait]` so that the dyn-dispatch boxing works (E0038 — fixed R33/L-227); (layer 2) the native-async SUPERTRAITS (`Tool`, `Runnable`) that are bridged to those façades via blanket impls must carry `+ Send` RPITIT on their async methods (E0277 — fixed R34/L-228). Fixing layer 1 exposes layer 2: once `#[async_trait]` is on the façade, the blanket impl now requires the supertrait's future to be provably Send, which it is NOT unless `+ Send` is explicit on the RPITIT. The PRODUCTION-GRADE FIX is to add `+ Send` bounds explicitly on the RPITIT: `async fn invoke(...) -> ... where Self: Send` or (preferred) `fn invoke(...) -> impl Future<Output = ...> + Send`. This mirrors the existing `VectorStoreFactory::from_texts_sync` precedent in the codebase. AUDIT DISCIPLINE: when auditing a realizability class (e.g., "does this trait work behind `dyn`?"), trace the FULL type-composition chain — supertrait → blanket impl → façade → dyn usage. Auditing only the immediate declaration (layer 1) misses the supertrait chain (layer 2). EXTENDS L-227 (comprehensive-class-audit discipline). PROCESS-GAP CANDIDATE: OBJECT-SAFETY-AUDIT-GATE layer-2 clause (already added to Drift/Deferrals per D-306). | Object-safe async dispatch is a MULTI-LAYER realizability stack: layer 1 = façade carries #[async_trait] (E0038); layer 2 = native-async supertraits bridged via blanket impls carry `+ Send` RPITIT so future is provably Send (E0277); fixing layer 1 exposes layer 2; audit full chain (supertrait → blanket impl → façade → dyn usage); extends L-227 comprehensive-class-audit discipline | Codified (F-P2A144-01[HIGH]/round-34/D-306, blanket DynTool/DynRunnable impl non-realizable E0277, Runnable::invoke/stream/batch `+ Send` RPITIT fix, VectorStoreFactory::from_texts_sync precedent, ADR-005 §Send-Bounded-RPITIT subsection, interface-definitions.md §Runnable-interface, 2026-08-29) |
| L-229 | **[PROCESS-GAP / CALL-DIRECTION-CONFORMANCE — BC/STORY SEAM SENTENCES MUST MATCH CONTAINMENT DIRECTION IN THE CANONICAL ADR]** [codified] Round-35 (F-P2A151-01 [MED]) found that BC-2.09.008 {PC-004} opening clause inverted the canonical seam: the clause made `CompiledStateGraph::invoke` the CALLER running the graph via `GraphRunner::run` — but all canonical references (ADR-029 §Decision 2/3/5, interface-definitions.md §GraphRunner, S-2.11 AC-020) agree that `GraphRunner::run` WRAPS `CompiledStateGraph::invoke`, not the other way around. The inversion survived ~35 adversary rounds because: (1) a correct trailing v2.0 note in the SAME postcondition stated the correct direction two sentences below; (2) prior sweeps verified type names, return value shapes, and method signatures — but NOT the grammatical subject/object direction of the seam sentence ("X runs the graph via Y" vs "Y wraps X"). STRUCTURAL LESSON: when auditing a seam described in a BC/story/ADR, verify the GRAMMATICAL DIRECTION of every "runs/wraps/calls/invokes" statement against the canonical ADR seam declaration. The canonical ADR is the authoritative source for containment direction: the ADR subject = caller/wrapper, the ADR object = callee/wrapped. A BC postcondition stating "callee does caller-action" is an inversion even if all named types, method signatures, and return values are correct. Audit protocol extension: after verifying type correctness, ALSO check: (a) which entity is the grammatical subject of the sentence (who is doing the action?); (b) does that match the containment direction in the ADR (who calls whom?); (c) if the sentence's subject != the canonical caller, the direction is inverted — fix regardless of whether the trailing details are correct. This class of defect — correct trailing note, inverted opening clause — is particularly dangerous because reviewers tend to scan for factual correctness of the named types rather than the relational direction of the opening sentence. PROCESS-GAP CANDIDATE: when a BC/story cites a seam (X runs/wraps/calls Y), a companion check should verify the grammatical direction against the governing ADR § that declares the containment. | BC/story seam sentences must match containment direction in canonical ADR; prior sweeps verified types/return-values but NOT the subject/object direction of the seam sentence; inverted opening clause (callee named as caller) can survive with correct trailing note; audit protocol must include grammatical subject-check against ADR containment direction | Codified (F-P2A151-01[MED]/round-35/D-307, BC-2.09.008 {PC-004} call-direction inversion corrected, ADR-029 §Decision 2/3/5 + interface-definitions.md §GraphRunner + S-2.11 AC-020 as canonical seam references, 2026-08-29) |
| L-226 | **[PROCESS-GAP / ADR-CANON SELF-CONSISTENCY — DECISION SUMMARY MUST MATCH OWN DETAILED SECTION AND HIGHER-PRECEDENCE PRD SUPPLEMENT]** [codified] Round-32 (F-P2A136-01 [HIGH]) found that ADR-025 §Decision item 3 (the D-48 Alternatives row) stated a non-compilable, infallible `as_retriever` return type `-> Arc<dyn VectorStoreRetriever + Send + Sync>` in the DECISION SUMMARY — while ADR-025's own detailed §as_retriever section was internally correct (fallible, `Result<VectorStoreRetriever, PregolyaError>`), and the higher-precedence interface-definitions.md also carried the correct form. ADR-025 is the type-signature authority for the corpus (POL-18), making its Decision summary the most-cited region of the doc. The defect had two independent violations: (1) `dyn VectorStoreRetriever` is E0782 because VectorStoreRetriever is a concrete `pub struct` — `dyn` requires a trait-object target, not a struct; (2) the infallible return contradicted ADR-025's own §as_retriever section and the higher-precedence interface-definitions.md. The pattern: when an ADR designated as canonical authority (per POL-18 or similar) contains both a DECISION SUMMARY row and a DETAILED subsection on the same decision item, these two sources MUST be byte-consistent on every type-signature, fallibility claim, and error variant. STRUCTURAL RULE: when editing a canonical ADR's detailed subsection (e.g., §as_retriever, §as_filter, §invoke_dyn), perform an explicit cross-check against: (a) the corresponding Decision summary row(s) in the same ADR (check for dyn, infallible, wrong error type); (b) the corresponding higher-precedence PRD supplement (interface-definitions.md §AsRetriever or §VectorStore) for the same API surface. If any of the three disagree, fix all three in the same burst. | L-241 | **[PROCESS-GAP / DI-SEAM-TYPE-UN-HOMED-IN-MODULE-DECOMPOSITION — INVOCATIONCONTEXT NAMED IN BC PRECONDITIONS FOR 48 ROUNDS WITH NO MODULE-DECOMPOSITION HOME; PHANTOM TYPE FROM IMPLEMENTATION PERSPECTIVE]** [codified] Round-49 (D-326) F-P2A207-02[HIGH] found that `InvocationContext` was referenced in BC-2.11.001 and BC-2.11.002 preconditions as the type the server passes to guardrail hook invocations, but had NO assigned home in `module-decomposition.md` (no module row, no creating story, no Arc-DI constructor assigned). This state persisted for 48 rounds. From the implementation perspective, `InvocationContext` was a phantom type — defined in BC text, making it "present" in the spec perimeter, but without a module-decomposition row the test-writer and implementer have no canonical location to define it, no crate assignment, and no story to create it. The type's phantom status was masked because: (1) BC precondition text refers to types by name without requiring a module-decomposition cross-reference, (2) the type name is sufficiently self-descriptive to be skipped in perimeter reviews, and (3) no failing test yet exercised the code path that would require the module to exist. STRUCTURAL LESSON: Any type named in BC preconditions as a DI object ("the server provides X") MUST have BOTH: (a) a `module-decomposition.md` row with crate home and creating story, AND (b) a corresponding row in `module-criticality.md`. These two rows are the mechanical markers that convert a phantom type into a buildable artifact. The product-owner/architect authoring DI-seam types in BC preconditions must simultaneously author the module-decomposition row — same burst, not a later pass. | L-243 | **[PROCESS-GAP / ADR-INTERNAL-BC-RESERVATION-HANDOFF-MISMATCH — AN ADR'S INTERNAL BC-RESERVATION TABLE AND ITS HANDOFF SUMMARY MUST AGREE; SUBSYSTEM AND BC-ID MUST BE CONSISTENT END-TO-END]** [codified] D-327 authoring burst: The Stage-1 architect (ADR-030 author) reserved 6 BCs in the ADR's internal §Subsystem-to-BC-Reservation table but reported only 5 BCs in the handoff summary narrative. This produced a discrepancy that was surfaced only when the product-owner began authoring BC files: the ADR body listed BC-2.04.011 (Trajectory Compaction Isolation) under SS-04, while the handoff summary had described a 6th BC as belonging to a different subsystem/slot (BC-2.02.009 PromoteRetireChannel). The confusion was resolvable by reading the ADR body carefully (ADR body is authoritative over handoff summary), but it caused friction and could have led to a misassigned BC. STRUCTURAL LESSON: An ADR authoring BCs on behalf of downstream specialists must maintain EXACT agreement between: (a) the internal BC-reservation table in the ADR body, (b) the subsystem-assignment of each reserved BC-ID, and (c) the handoff summary count and ID list. Any discrepancy between these three is a defect in the ADR, not an open question for the product-owner to adjudicate. The handoff summary is derived from the ADR body — if the counts disagree, the ADR body is the authoritative source and the handoff summary must be corrected before handoff. PREVENTION: After authoring BC-reservation rows in an ADR, the author MUST: (1) count the rows and verify the count matches the summary, (2) list the BC-IDs explicitly in the summary, (3) verify each BC-ID maps to the correct subsystem (SS-NN prefix matches the subsystem in the table). | D-327 ADR-030 internal BC-reservation table reported 6 BCs but handoff summary implied 5; BC-2.04.011 (Trajectory Compaction Isolation SS-04) vs BC-2.02.009 (PromoteRetireChannel SS-02) subsystem/BC-ID mismatch surfaced at PO authoring time; ADR body is authoritative; handoff summary count and BC-ID list must agree with ADR body BC-reservation table | Codified (D-327 authoring burst, 2026-08-31; ADR-030 authoring; BC-2.02.009/BC-2.04.011 disambiguation) |
