| L-250 | **[OPERATIONAL / DO-NOT-SPEC-UNREACHABLE-ERROR-PATHS — STRUCTURALLY-UNREACHABLE-DEAD-GUARD IN BC PRECONDITION IS A DEFECT, NOT A SAFETY MARGIN]** [codified] Round-52 (D-330): P2A-216 F-P2A216-01[HIGH] found that BC-2.04.011 {PC-005}/{INV-004} specifies E-TRAJ-004 TrajectoryRetainedEligible for a guard condition that is structurally unreachable at runtime — `TrajectoryRetentionPolicy` derives eligible and retained as strict complements by construction (mutual exclusion at the type level). A record cannot simultaneously be retained and eligible; the Rust type system prevents the state at compile time. Despite this, a BC precondition was authored for the case, an error code was minted, and TV-003 was authored as a test vector for the unreachable path. The unreachable test vector provides false coverage confidence — it counts toward TV census but can never be triggered in production. STRUCTURAL LESSON: Before authoring a BC precondition that specifies a guard condition on a DI-seam or trait type, the author must verify the condition is REACHABLE by construction. If the trait or type enforces mutual exclusion (complements, sealed enums, builder patterns that enforce invariants), the guard condition is a specification defect. Dead-guard error codes in the error taxonomy are phantoms — they accumulate census weight and require unreachable match arms in implementations. PREVENTION: For any precondition of the form "if X and Y are simultaneously true, raise E-NNN": verify that X and Y can be simultaneously true given the type design. If not, retire the precondition before commit; do not author the error code; do not mint a TV. | Round-52 F-P2A216-01[HIGH] BC-2.04.011 {PC-005}/{INV-004} E-TRAJ-004 unreachable dead-guard path (TrajectoryRetentionPolicy eligible/retained are complements by type construction); E-TRAJ-004 RETIRED; TV-003 removed; E-TRAJ-005 MINTED (DURABILITY class; real SQLite I/O failure path); EC net-neutral (142 total) | Codified (round-52 / D-330, 2026-08-31; P2A-216 realizability F-P2A216-01[HIGH]; product-owner B BC-2.04.011; E-TRAJ-004 tombstone; E-TRAJ-005 MINTED; TV-003 removed; TV-005+TV-006 minted) |
| L-251 | **[OPERATIONAL / DI-SEAM-ARC-DYN-MUST-REFERENCE-DEFINED-OBJECT-SAFE-TRAIT — INTERFACE-DEFINITIONS-MUST-DEFINE-TRAIT-BEFORE-BC-REFERENCES-IT]** [codified] Round-52 (D-330): P2A-216 F-P2A216-02[HIGH] + P2A-217 F-P2A217-03[MED] found that BC-2.04.009 cited an `Arc<dyn Serializer>` DI seam (DI-001) but `Serializer` was not defined anywhere in the spec corpus — not in interface-definitions.md, not in any architecture doc, not as a type in any BC. The DI-seam reference was notional: BC text said "when a Serializer is configured" without providing a trait definition. From an implementer's perspective, the trait does not exist — there is no object-safe method signature to implement against, no crate home, no BC or story that creates the type. BC authoring a DI seam via `Arc<dyn X>` while X has no definition is equivalent to a dead guard path: the seam cannot be wired. STRUCTURAL LESSON: Any BC that specifies `Arc<dyn Trait>` DI wiring MUST be authored simultaneously with: (a) the trait definition in interface-definitions.md (full method signatures; object-safe; crate home assigned), (b) a module-decomposition row for the trait, and (c) a story whose File Structure section includes the trait source file. These three are the mechanical markers that convert a notional DI seam into a buildable artifact. The BC cannot precede the trait definition — if the trait is not yet defined, the DI seam is a phantom. CLOSURE: interface-definitions v3.07 — `core::serializer::Serializer` trait defined with serialize/deserialize methods; object-safe; DI-001 seam formalized. | Round-52 F-P2A216-02[HIGH]+F-P2A217-03[MED] BC-2.04.009 DI-001 Arc<dyn Serializer> seam cited but Serializer trait undefined; closure: interface-definitions v3.07 core::serializer::Serializer trait definition (object-safe; DI-001 seam formalized); concurrent with plaintext-comparison semantics update | Codified (round-52 / D-330, 2026-08-31; P2A-216/P2A-217 realizability/security; architect A; interface-definitions v3.07; BC-2.04.009 {INV-001}/{INV-002}; TV-005+TV-006) |

The harness-specification in a VP is the executable contract — if it references an API that no longer exists post-redesign, the harness is broken on arrival and cannot satisfy Phase-6 formal verification. STRUCTURAL LESSON: Any architect burst that redesigns a trait or DI seam MUST simultaneously update all VP harness-specifications that reference that seam's API. The VP harness is not cosmetic documentation — it is the Kani/fuzz proof scaffold, and stale API references in it represent concrete compilation failures at Phase-6. The fix is not "update the harness later" — it must be in the same burst as the API redesign. The rule generalizes: when a type signature, method name, or enum variant changes, grep for all VP files that reference the old signature (`rg 'old_method_name' .factory/specs/verification-properties/`) and update them in-scope. | L-253 | **[PROCESS-GAP / RIPGREP-FACTORY-BLIND-SPOT — `rg` SILENTLY SKIPS `.factory/` ORPHAN-BRANCH WORKTREE BECAUSE IT IS GITIGNORED]** [codified] Round-53 (D-332): P2A-223 surfaced as a PROCESS-GAP finding that agents running corpus-wide `rg` (ripgrep) searches from the project root silently skip `.factory/` because the orphan-branch worktree is gitignored by the main repo. Ripgrep respects `.gitignore` by default; the `.factory/` directory is in `.gitignore` (it is a separate orphan-branch worktree, not a tracked subdirectory of develop). This means a command like `rg 'DI-014' .` issued from the project root returns no results from `.factory/specs/` even when DI-014 is present — without any warning. This blind spot caused VP-017 DI-014 drift to persist undetected across three index files (VP-INDEX, ARCH-INDEX, BC-INDEX VP Seed BCs table) that were not caught by standard corpus sweeps. The adversary's P2A-223 deep-audit pass discovered the issue by explicitly targeting `.factory/` paths. STRUCTURAL LESSON: All corpus-wide grep/rg commands intended to search `.factory/` spec files MUST use one of: (a) explicit absolute path (`rg 'pattern' /absolute/path/to/.factory/`), (b) explicit relative path from project root (`rg 'pattern' .factory/`), (c) `rg --no-ignore 'pattern'` from project root, or (d) `git -C .factory grep 'pattern'`. The bare `rg 'pattern' .` form from the project root MUST NOT be used in corpus-wide sweeps — it silently skips `.factory/` contents. FOLLOW-UP: orchestrator+devops to document the `--no-ignore` / explicit-path convention in the factory sweep discipline; candidate for a devops story in first-self-improvement-wave. NOT a silent deferral — explicit owner orchestrator+devops; follow-up story target is first-self-improvement-wave. | Round-53 P2A-223 PG-P2A223-01: `rg 'DI-014' .` silent skip of `.factory/` worktree; VP-017 DI-014 drift present in VP-INDEX/ARCH-INDEX/BC-INDEX VP Seed BCs table undetected by standard sweep; remediation: explicit `.factory/` path or `--no-ignore` flag; follow-up: corpus-sweep convention in devops discipline | Codified (round-53 / D-332, 2026-08-31; P2A-223 process-gap PG-P2A223-01; owner orchestrator+devops; first-self-improvement-wave) |

| L-257 | **[OPERATIONAL / DERIVE-DEFAULT-OVER-BOUNDS-GENERIC-MARKERS — `#[derive(Default)]` ON A STRUCT GENERIC OVER T EMITS SPURIOUS `T: Default` BOUND EVEN WHEN T APPEARS ONLY IN PHANTOMDATA]** [codified] Round-56 (D-335): F-P2A227-01[HIGH] found that `#[derive(Default)]` on `LedgerChannel<T>` and `PromoteRetireChannel<T>` — where T appears only in `PhantomData<T>` — emits a spurious `T: Default` bound (rustc#26925), breaking a `Channel: Default` supertrait obligation. The canonical fix is a MANUAL bound-free `impl Default`. This bug class was ALREADY KNOWN: interface-definitions round-40/F-P2A168-03 documented the same `PhantomData<fn(I)->O>` case. Yet rounds 53/54 re-introduced the defect by mandating derive-only for the same pattern — a canon-regression that the fresh-context adversary caught. STRUCTURAL LESSON: Any struct that is generic over T where T appears ONLY in PhantomData MUST use a manual bound-free `impl Default { fn default() -> Self { Self { _marker: PhantomData } } }` — NEVER `#[derive(Default)]` for this class of type. The precedent is established; citing the prior lesson (L-xxx PhantomData fn type) is not sufficient — the manual impl requirement must be embedded in the ADR §Decision and interface-definitions §Type simultaneously. Re-introduction via derive-only canon mandate is the recurrence pattern: when an ADR Decision section says "use #[derive(Default)]" without carving out the PhantomData-only case, the mandate is wrong for that case. | L-259 | **[PROCESS-GAP / CLEAN-STRICT-IS-NOT-CONVERGENCE — 3-CLEAN PROTOCOL REQUIREMENT FOR THREE INDEPENDENT DIVERSE-LENS PASSES IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT CEREMONY]** [codified] Round-56 (D-335): P2A-226 passed CLEAN(strict)=YES (streak advanced to 1/3). On the very next independent pass P2A-227 (different adversary lens), 2 HIGH findings were uncovered, resetting streak to 0/3. This mirrors the structure of prior near-convergences that reset: a single CLEAN pass proves the adversary could not find issues under ONE specific lens on ONE frozen HEAD, but does NOT prove the corpus is clean. The 3-CLEAN protocol requires three independent passes with diverse lenses, none of which finds a single issue. STRUCTURAL LESSON: Streak advancement (1/3, 2/3) is a PROGRESS SIGNAL, not a convergence signal. P2A-226 CLEAN(strict) at 1/3 does NOT mean the corpus is spec-ready — it means one lens found no issues. The next diverse-lens pass is not a formality; it is an independent evidence-gathering event. The BC-5.39.001 3-CLEAN requirement for THREE independent diverse-lens passes is the minimal bar for convergence confidence; single-clean is insufficient evidence of spec correctness. Counter-pattern: treating 1/3 as "almost converged" and dispatching a lightweight next pass — the diverse lens in P2A-227 found HIGH findings that invalidated the 1/3 streak. | L-261 | **[PROCESS-GAP / ASSOCIATED-TYPE BOUND COMPLETENESS — WHEN A TRAIT DECLARES BOUNDS ON AN ASSOCIATED TYPE, EVERY IMPL'S CONCRETE ASSOCIATED TYPE MUST SATISFY THOSE BOUNDS; USER ENUMS DO NOT AUTO-DERIVE CLONE]** [codified] Round-58 (D-337): F-P2A229-01[MED] found that PromoteRetireOp<T> — the concrete `type Update` in the PromoteRetireChannel Channel impl — lacked `#[derive(Clone, Debug)]` required by the `Channel: type Update: Clone + Send + Sync + 'static` bound. The round-56 fix correctly added manual `impl Default` for PromoteRetireOp<T> and LedgerChannel<T> but did NOT simultaneously audit whether Clone (the other Update bound) was also satisfied. The derive-Clone case differs from the manual-Default case: `#[derive(Clone)]` IS sound here because LedgerEntry⊃Clone and PhantomData<T: Clone> is Clone, and there is no rustc#26925 spurious-bound problem for Clone (only for Default and other non-bundled traits). STRUCTURAL LESSON: Realizability review must enumerate ALL trait bound obligations — supertrait bounds (e.g., `Channel: Default`) AND associated-type bounds (`Update: Clone`, `Accumulator: ...`) AND method-signature bounds — against EACH concrete impl, not just the first obligation examined. The round-56 review found and fixed the Default obligation; the Clone obligation was present in the same `Channel::Update` bound declaration but was not checked. COMPANION to L-257 (Default lesson): `Default` is not in the LedgerEntry auto-derive bundle (needs manual bound-free impl per rustc#26925) whereas `Clone` IS in the bundle (`#[derive(Clone)]` is correct) — the resolution differs per-bound depending on whether the required trait is covered by the element's supertrait. PREVENTION: When fixing a trait bound realization gap, run a mechanical checklist: for the affected impl block, list ALL bounds on ALL associated types and ALL supertrait bounds, and verify each one independently in the same burst. | L-262 | **[PROCESS-GAP / OPS-ARTIFACT SIBLING-SET COMPLETENESS — ADDING OR REMOVING A STORY MUST SWEEP MACHINE-FACING DISPATCH ARTIFACTS, NOT ONLY HUMAN-FACING INDICES]** [codified] Round-60 (D-339): P2A-232 found that the D-327 praxist CAP-040 authoring burst correctly updated the human-facing story indices (STORY-INDEX, epics.md, HS-INDEX) but left the machine-facing dispatch artifacts stale for ~30 adversarial rounds: sprint-state.yaml had no blocks for S-1.28 or S-2.12 (F-P2A232-01 HIGH); wave-schedule.md showed 39 stories instead of 41 (F-P2A232-02 HIGH); dependency-graph.md was missing the S-1.10 blocks-S-2.12 reverse edge (F-P2A232-03 HIGH); dependency-graph.md VP-to-Stories matrix had only 19 VP rows instead of 20 (F-P2A232-04 MED). The root cause is that the new-surface-authoring self-check (L-244) covers human-facing index census (STORY-INDEX/BC-INDEX/VP-INDEX/HS-INDEX) but does NOT include the machine-facing dispatch artifacts: sprint-state.yaml, wave-schedule.md, dependency-graph.md reverse-edges (blocks), and the VP-to-Stories matrix. STRUCTURAL LESSON: Adding or removing a story MUST sweep ALL four categories of artifact simultaneously in the SAME authoring burst: (1) human-facing story indices (STORY-INDEX census table, header blockquote, BC-coverage-map intro; epics.md epic point total; HS-INDEX holdout count if stories anchor holdout scenarios); (2) machine-facing dispatch artifacts (sprint-state.yaml per-story blocks; wave-schedule.md sub-batch row); (3) dependency-graph.md bidirectional edges — for EVERY depends_on relation added in the story frontmatter, the reverse blocks entry MUST be added to the blocking story's blocks list in the SAME commit; and (4) the VP-to-Stories matrix in dependency-graph.md — for every VP in the story's verification_properties frontmatter, a VP-to-Stories matrix row MUST be added. The authoring burst self-check (L-244) is extended here to explicitly include these four machine-facing artifact categories. FOLLOW-UP RECOMMENDATION (orchestrator + devops): add a mechanical cross-artifact story-membership consistency hook that verifies every story appears consistently across {STORY-INDEX, dependency-graph.md story-batch section, wave-schedule.md sub-batch, sprint-state.yaml} and that all depends_on / blocks edges are symmetric. This hook would convert the OPS-ARTIFACT SIBLING-SET class from adversary-caught to mechanically-caught at commit time. Tag this lesson as a first-self-improvement-wave follow-up per the Cycle-Closing Checklist. NOT a silent defer — explicit follow-up target (first-self-improvement-wave) and owner (devops-engineer) assigned. | D-327 CAP-040 authoring burst updated STORY-INDEX/epics/HS-INDEX (human-facing) but left sprint-state.yaml/wave-schedule.md (39→41 stories)/dependency-graph.md (missing S-1.10-blocks-S-2.12 reverse edge + VP-019 matrix row) stale for ~30 rounds until P2A-232 structural-lens audit caught it; extends L-244 self-check to include machine-facing dispatch artifacts; follow-up: mechanical cross-artifact story-membership consistency hook | Codified (round-60 / D-339, 2026-09-01; P2A-232 machine-dispatch-artifact audit; F-P2A232-01[HIGH,POL-8]+F-P2A232-02[HIGH]+F-P2A232-03[HIGH]+F-P2A232-04[MED]; sprint-state.yaml + wave-schedule.md + dependency-graph.md ops-artifact propagation; extends L-244 new-surface-authoring self-check; follow-up devops hook recommendation) |
| L-263 | **[PROCESS-GAP / MECHANISM-PIVOT SECOND-ORDER SWEEP — WHEN A COMPACTION OR BATCH-WRITE MECHANISM CHANGES, VERIFY THAT EVERY BC THAT DESCRIBES THAT MECHANISM REFLECTS THE NEW MODEL; PRIMARY BC IS INSUFFICIENT]** [codified] Round-62 (D-340): F-P2A234-01[HIGH] found that the original staging-swap compaction pattern in BC-2.04.011 was catastrophically incorrect for concurrent-run workloads: the staging table accumulation was per-compactor, not per-run, so a compactor serving run A would silently delete records from run B. The fix redesigned the mechanism to per-run single-txn DELETE scoped to single run_id. STRUCTURAL LESSON: When a mechanism redesign changes the atomicity scope or error-surface of a bulk write operation (compaction, batch upsert, archive-swap), the fix-burst MUST sweep ALL BCs that directly model that operation's invariants (BC-2.04.011 in this case) AND all BCs that model CALLERS of that operation that rely on its isolation guarantees (BC-2.04.009 put_record in this case, which shared the same incorrect concurrency claim). A mechanism-pivot is NOT complete when the primary mechanism BC is updated; it is complete when all caller/peer BCs that model invariants enabled by the mechanism have been re-verified against the new model. ALSO: concurrent-safety claims for SQLite BEGIN IMMEDIATE must be stated accurately — BEGIN IMMEDIATE serializes ALL writers, not just concurrent writers within a single run. PREVENTION: When closing a mechanism-pivot finding, add an explicit step to the fix-burst prompt: "grep all BC files for references to this operation and verify each one's invariants hold under the new mechanism model." | F-P2A234-01[HIGH] BC-2.04.011 staging-swap mechanism catastrophically wrong for concurrent runs; per-run single-txn DELETE scoped to run_id; F-P2A234-02[HIGH] BC-2.04.009 false BEGIN IMMEDIATE concurrent-safety claim; mechanism-pivot must sweep all caller/peer BCs; companion to L-256 anchor-propagation sweep discipline | Codified (round-62 / D-340, 2026-09-01; F-P2A234-01[HIGH]+F-P2A234-02[HIGH]; BC-2.04.011 per-run DELETE + BC-2.04.009 BEGIN IMMEDIATE; mechanism-pivot second-order sweep; ADR-030 §Compaction Atomicity Decision) |
| L-264 | **[PROCESS-GAP / FALLIBLE-PATH COMPLETENESS — EVERY OPERATION WITH A STAGING PHASE NEEDS AN ERROR CODE FOR STAGING FAILURE; A SPEC THAT DEFINES SUCCESS AND COMMIT-FAILURE BUT NOT STAGING-FAILURE IS INCOMPLETE]** [codified] Round-62 (D-340): F-P2A234-03[MED] found that the error taxonomy did not include an error code for the staging-phase failure of trajectory compaction: the original design defined E-TRAJ-005 (TrajectoryCompactionFailed for commit-phase failure) but no code for pre-commit staging failures (e.g., staging DELETE rows not applied, wrong run_id scope). The fix minted E-TRAJ-006 TrajectoryCompactionStagingFailed. STRUCTURAL LESSON: Any operation that has a multi-phase structure (stage, validate, commit) needs a distinct error code for EACH phase where failure is observable to the caller. "Catch-all" single-error coverage is insufficient when the staging phase can fail independently of the commit phase. When authoring a BC that describes a multi-phase operation, the error taxonomy section MUST enumerate: (a) failure at each distinct phase, (b) whether partial state persists after each failure mode, (c) the expected recovery path from each error. A BC that covers success + final-commit-failure but omits staging-failure is an incomplete error surface specification. PREVENTION: When authoring BCs for multi-phase operations, use a checklist: enumerate all phases, enumerate all phase-failure error codes, check error-taxonomy.md for each one. | F-P2A234-03[MED] error taxonomy missing E-TRAJ-006 staging-phase failure; E-TRAJ-005 covered commit-phase but not staging-phase of the per-run DELETE compaction; multi-phase operations need per-phase error codes; prevents "catch-all" error surface gaps | Codified (round-62 / D-340, 2026-09-01; F-P2A234-03[MED] E-TRAJ-006 TrajectoryCompactionStagingFailed; error-taxonomy §E-TRAJ-006; EC 142→143; multi-phase operation error coverage discipline) |
| L-265 | **[PROCESS-GAP / COVERAGE PARITY — A BEHAVIORAL CONTRACT WITH TWO INVARIANTS REQUIRES A FORMAL VERIFICATION PROPERTY THAT EXERCISES BOTH; SINGLE-INV VP COVERAGE ON A TWO-INV BC IS PARTIAL]** [codified] Round-62 (D-340): F-P2A234-04[MED] found that BC-2.02.009 had two invariants — {INV-001} idempotency and {INV-002} ordering — but no VP covered either. The fix minted VP-020 as a proptest P1 covering both invariants jointly (promote_retire_channel_idempotency harness). STRUCTURAL LESSON: A BC with N independently testable invariants needs at least ONE VP or test that exercises each invariant path. "Coverage parity" means every INV declared in a BC body has a traceable test anchor (AC in a story or a VP). When authoring a new BC, the post-authoring self-check MUST verify: for each {INV-NNN} declared in the BC, is there either (a) an AC in an anchoring story that exercises that INV, or (b) a VP in VP-INDEX that cites that INV? If neither exists, the VP is pending and must be filed. FOLLOW-UP PATTERN: The coverage parity check is now a standing adversary probe (SAP-1 extension): "for every new BC with 2+ invariants, verify VP and/or story AC coverage for each invariant." | F-P2A234-04[MED] BC-2.02.009 had {INV-001}+{INV-002} with no VP; VP-020 proptest P1 minted covering both; coverage parity rule: each BC invariant needs a traceable test anchor (AC or VP); companion to the VP seeding discipline from L-243 | Codified (round-62 / D-340, 2026-09-01; F-P2A234-04[MED] VP-020 PromoteRetireChannel idempotency/ordering; BC-2.02.009 {INV-001}+{INV-002}; VP 20→21; coverage parity: per-invariant test anchor requirement) |
| L-266 | **[PROCESS-GAP / NEGATIVE-SPACE LENS VALUE — THE ADVERSARIAL "WHAT IS NOT SPECIFIED" LENS CATCHES ENTIRE MISSING ERROR CODES, VPs, AND NFRs THAT POSITIVE-COVERAGE LENSES CANNOT SEE]** [codified] Round-62 (D-340): Three of the five substantive findings (F-P2A234-03[MED] E-TRAJ-006, F-P2A234-04[MED] VP-020, F-P2A234-05[MED] NFR-015) were all instances of the same finding class: something that SHOULD EXIST in the spec does not exist. These are "negative-space" findings — they are invisible to lenses that check whether existing content is correct, and can only be found by asking "what is missing?" STRUCTURAL LESSON: Adversarial reviews MUST include an explicit negative-space pass that asks: (1) For every operation with observable failure modes, does the error taxonomy have a code for each failure mode? (2) For every BC with multiple independently testable invariants, does VP-INDEX have a VP for each invariant? (3) For every system capability with measurable durability, availability, or performance expectations, does the NFR catalog have an entry? These three questions together form the "negative-space lens" — a standing adversary discipline. The positive-coverage lenses (does each BC body correctly describe the mechanism? are all claims accurate?) cannot catch these absences because there is no wrong text to flag. PREVENTION: Add an explicit "negative-space lens" pass to the adversary dispatch prompt for Phase-2 story/spec reviews: after the positive-coverage review, ask the three negative-space questions above and file findings for each gap. | F-P2A234-03[MED]+F-P2A234-04[MED]+F-P2A234-05[MED] all negative-space findings (E-TRAJ-006 missing, VP-020 missing, NFR-015 missing); positive-coverage lenses cannot detect absences; negative-space lens is a standing adversary probe: (1) per-phase error codes, (2) per-invariant VP coverage, (3) per-capability NFR entry | Codified (round-62 / D-340, 2026-09-01; 3/5 substantive P2A-234 findings all negative-space class; E-TRAJ-006+VP-020+NFR-015; negative-space lens standing adversary probe discipline; companion to L-264 and L-265) |
| L-267 | **[PROCESS-GAP / ADR-SELF-CONSISTENCY SWEEP — AN ADR THAT INTRODUCES A NEW MECHANISM MUST BE VERIFIED FOR INTERNAL CONSISTENCY ACROSS ALL ITS SECTIONS BEFORE MARKING AS ACCEPTED; DIRECTIVE AND DECISION-BODY MUST AGREE]** [codified] Round-63 (D-341): F-P2A235-01[HIGH] found that ADR-030 §SQLite Topology contained two contradictory deletion mechanisms for compaction: (a) the original multi-pass DELETE accumulation pattern (from the initial draft) and (b) the per-run single-txn DELETE mechanism (introduced by the round-62 redesign). Both were present in the same ADR section simultaneously. The contradiction survived the round-62 fix-burst because the round-62 burst updated the Decision section to per-run DELETE but did not simultaneously audit whether the Topology section still referenced the old mechanism. STRUCTURAL LESSON: When an ADR section is edited to reflect a mechanism change, the fix-burst MUST include a mechanical self-consistency sweep: grep all sections of the ADR for the old mechanism pattern and verify each one was updated or explicitly retired. An ADR's §Decision, §Consequences, §Topology/Implementation, and §NFR-directives sections can each describe the mechanism independently — updating one without sweeping the others produces silent internal contradiction. PREVENTION: Add a mandatory ADR self-consistency sweep step to any fix-burst that changes a mechanism: after the primary section edit, grep the full ADR file for the old mechanism's canonical keyword (e.g., "per-compactor DELETE", "staging table accumulation") and verify zero occurrences remain in normative sections. | F-P2A235-01[HIGH] ADR-030 §SQLite Topology dual-mechanism contradiction — per-compactor accumulation pattern not removed when per-run single-txn DELETE redesign was committed in round-62; both present simultaneously; ADR self-consistency sweep is mandatory after any mechanism-pivot edit | Codified (round-63 / D-341, 2026-09-01; F-P2A235-01[HIGH] ADR-030 §SQLite Topology; per-run DELETE redesign; ADR self-consistency sweep discipline; companion to L-263 mechanism-pivot sweep) |
| L-268 | **[PROCESS-GAP / DIRECTIVE-ARTIFACT RECONCILIATION — WHEN AN ADR DIRECTIVE MANDATES A PRIORITY LEVEL FOR AN ARTIFACT (e.g., NFR), THAT ARTIFACT MUST BE UPDATED IN THE SAME BURST AS THE ADR; CROSS-DOCUMENT DIRECTIVES CREATE DEFERRED DRIFT WHEN NOT IMMEDIATELY RECONCILED]** [codified] Round-63 (D-341): F-P2A235-04[MED] found that ADR-030 §NFR-015 included an explicit directive: "NFR-015 MUST be P0 to match NFR-002 (put_writes durability)." The ADR directive was added in round-62. However, nfr-catalog.md §NFR-015 was still P1 — the priority had not been updated in the round-62 burst despite the ADR explicitly mandating P0. The root cause is that the round-62 burst authoring sequence updated the ADR to include the directive, but did not include nfr-catalog.md in the same burst's change set. STRUCTURAL LESSON: Any ADR edit that adds or modifies a directive targeting another artifact (e.g., "NFR-NNN MUST be P0", "BC-S.SS.NNN MUST include error-code E-NNN", "VP-NNN proof obligation is P1") MUST update the target artifact in the SAME burst or the directive creates immediate drift. The rule: an ADR directive is not authoritatively effective until the target artifact reflects it. Adding a directive to an ADR while leaving the target artifact contradicting the directive is an incomplete state that will be found as a MED finding in the next adversarial pass. PREVENTION: When authoring or editing an ADR, grep the full ADR text for directive keywords ("MUST be P", "MUST include", "proof obligation", "requires BC", "P0 parity") and for each directive, verify the named target artifact currently satisfies the directive — or update it in the same burst. | F-P2A235-04[MED] nfr-catalog §NFR-015 P1 while ADR-030 §NFR-015 directive mandates P0; ADR directive→artifact reconciliation must happen atomically; ADR §NFR-015 directive not propagated to nfr-catalog.md in same burst | Codified (round-63 / D-341, 2026-09-01; F-P2A235-04[MED] nfr-catalog §NFR-015 P1 vs ADR-030 §NFR-015 P0 directive; ADR directive-to-artifact reconciliation discipline; immediate propagation required) |
| L-269 | **[PROCESS-GAP / INPUT-HASH PER-FILE DISCIPLINE — WHEN AN INPUT FILE CHANGES, EVERY ARTIFACT THAT DECLARES THAT FILE IN ITS inputs: LIST MUST HAVE ITS INPUT-HASH REFRESHED VIA compute-input-hash --update; NEVER COPY HASHES BETWEEN FILES]** [codified] Round-63 (D-341): F-P2A235-07[MED] found that BC-2.02.009 had a stale input-hash (e5c46b2) pointing to a hash computed before ADR-030 was updated to v2.1 this burst. Extended sweep found that BC-2.02.007, BC-2.02.008, BC-2.04.010, VP-017, VP-018, VP-019, VP-020 all also had stale hashes — every file that declared ADR-030 in its inputs: list needed refreshing. This finding had been flagged once before (the task noted it was being flagged "for the second time"). STRUCTURAL LESSON: The input-hash is a per-file content commitment. When any file in an artifact's declared inputs: list changes, that artifact's input-hash must be refreshed by running `compute-input-hash <file> --update` on the artifact itself — not by copying another file's hash, even if both files happen to have identical inputs: lists. Two files with identical inputs: lists legitimately share the same hash (it is computed from the same source content) but the hash MUST be computed independently for each file, never copied. PREVENTION: At the end of every burst that modifies a shared input file (e.g., ADR-030, interface-definitions, invariants.md), run a mandatory sweep: `grep -r '<modified-input-filename>' .factory/specs/ --include="*.md" -l` to find all dependents, then run `compute-input-hash <each-dependent> --update` on each. This sweep converts input-hash drift from an adversary-caught class to a within-burst self-correction. | F-P2A235-07[MED] BC-2.02.009 stale input-hash e5c46b2 after ADR-030 §Compaction Atomicity Decision update; extended sweep found 7 additional stale hashes (BC-2.02.007/008, BC-2.04.010, VP-017/018/019/020); hashes flagged stale for the second consecutive adversarial round; per-file tool invocation required — never copy; mandatory post-input-change sweep discipline | Codified (round-63 / D-341, 2026-09-01; F-P2A235-07[MED] 8 stale input-hashes after ADR-030 §Compaction Atomicity Decision; compute-input-hash --update per-file; dependency sweep after shared-input changes; second recurrence of same finding class) |
